4
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
Summary
The year 2011 was marked by numerous events in the economic,
social, political sphere, as well as by natural disasters that
changed the world economic climate. Dramatic increases in
food and oil prices, high unemployment, high inﬂation rates,
and ﬁscal consolidation have caused disturbances in the eco-
nomic activities and created pressure on the consumers budgets.
Also, in the last quarters of 2011 were recorded signals on the
worsening global economy, the main focus being the sovereign
debt problem in developed economies, manifested mainly in the
euro area countries.
Asian countries were hit by the weakening global demand,
primarily Japan, strongly affected by the earthquake in March
2011. Emerging economies have recorded a relatively high
growth rate, but it was offset by the tightening of the mon-
etary policy, and by adverse effects caused by propagation of
international ﬁnancial instability.
World prices rose signiﬁcantly during 2011, their evolution
being positively inﬂuenced by the economic boom in China
and other Asian countries, by the energy prices increase due
to political tensions in the Middle East and North Africa, by
the disturbance in international products circuit following the
earthquake in March 2011 in Japan. For these reasons, during
the year, many central banks have tightened their monetary
policy, contributing to the reduction of inﬂationary pressures.
Moreover, some countries faced additional inﬂationary pressures
generated by the depreciation of the national currencies.
The Moldovan economy recorded a 6.4 percent growth dur-
ing 2011 as compared to the previous year, mainly due to
the economic recovery of the main trade partners. This fact
determined the upward evolution of exports, which increased
by 28.6 percent, generating this way the maximum positive
contribution to the GDP growth. At the same time, household
consumption rose by 8.5 percent, mainly due to the increase
of its ﬁnancing sources during the period, with a signiﬁcant
positive impact on GDP growth. The gross capital formation
increase had a positive contribution, but of a lower extend, while
government ﬁnal consumption registered a negative evolution
during the reference period. The increased domestic demand
contributed to the imports growth by 19.3 percent. In 2011,
positive developments were recorded in all sectors. Similar to


Summary
5
the previous year, the increase in gross value added of services-
related branches had the largest contribution to the GDP growth.
The industry recorded a growth rate below the level recorded
in 2010, and constructions, despite the positive developments
of the last two years, have not yet reached the level recorded
before the economic crisis.
At the end of 2011, the annual inﬂation rate constituted 7.8
percent, while the annual core inﬂation recorded a value of
6.1 percent in December 2011. Consequently, the difference
between the CPI and the core inﬂation rate was of 1.7 percent-
age points, thus further outlining the signiﬁcant inﬂuence of
the non-monetary factors on inﬂation process in the Republic
of Moldova. The prices for regulated services (2.7 percentage
points) contributed the most to the annual inﬂation formation
in December 2011, because of increases of tariffs for gas, elec-
tricity, and heating in the spring and fall of 2011. Food prices
contributed by 2.5 percentage points to the annual inﬂation rate
growth due to the propagation of the second round effects as a
result of increases in fuel prices and gas and electricity tariffs
at the beginning of 2011. At the same time, the higher prices
for food products were driven by adverse weather conditions
for speciﬁc crops and the increase in prices on foreign markets.
The goods and services related to core inﬂation had a major
contribution (1.9 percentage points), caused by second-round
effects, due to higher prices for regulated services, fuel and
food, as well as by the increasing demand from population in
the ﬁrst half of 2011. Fluctuations in oil prices on world market,
exacerbated by the tensions in the Middle East and North Africa
in early 2011, with liqueﬁed gas deﬁcit in the third quarter
of 2011, determined the increase of fuel prices, which have
contributed to the annual inﬂation growth by 0.7 percentage
points.
Quantifying the macroeconomic situation, trends and projec-
tions of macroeconomic indicators in the medium term, the
inﬂation outlook in short and medium term, with possible
uncertainties and challenges during the reference period, as
well as to anchor inﬂationary pressures, the National Bank of
Moldova has promoted a prudent monetary policy during 2011.
The NBM’s response to increased inﬂationary pressures in the pe-
riod of January - November 2011 was to progressively increase
the tightening of the interest rate policy, initiated in January
2011. Thus, the NBM operated three upward adjustments of
the monetary policy rate, its level was increased from 7.0 to
10.0 percent. At the same time, the mitigation of price increases
by the end of 2011 and the high degree of uncertainty in the
global economy have prompted the central bank to loosen the
monetary policy, initiating in November 2011 a prudent trend


6
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
of reduction of monetary policy interest rate by performing two
subsequent reductions, with an amplitude of 0.5 percentage
points and 1.0 percentage points respectively (the latter being
applied from January 6, 2012).
The components of monetary policy operational framework, spe-
ciﬁc for the strategy to reduce inﬂation, have been extensively
applied in 2011. The 2011 situation, characterized by liquidity
excess in the money market and inﬂationary pressures increase
has driven the National Bank of Moldova to act as a net debtor
in relation to the banking system.
In order to increase the effectiveness of money market liquidity
management, the NBM increased, starting from January 2011,
the maturity on NBM certiﬁcates from one to two weeks.
In 2011, the average weighted rate of sterilization operations
amounted to 8.54 percent annually compared to 6.74 percent
recorded in the previous year.
In order to counter the inﬂationary pressures and to improve
the monetary policy decisions’ transmission mechanism of the
National Bank, in the context of an underdeveloped capital mar-
ket and abundant liquidity in the banking system, the required
reserves ratio was increased twice in 2011 by 3.0 percentage
points each time, reaching the level of 14.0 percent at the end of
2011 from the funds attracted in MDL, non-convertible currency,
and freely convertible currency.
In 2011, the required reserves mechanism continued to exercise
the functions of monetary control and banking system liquidity
management. During the required reserves regime undergone
signiﬁcant changes in terms of increasing the tracking periods
of attracted funds and required reserves maintenance periods,
rising the required reserves ratio, and applying the 0% required
reserves ratio of attracted funds included in the calculation base,
with a maturity greater than two years.
Since January 2011, according to the Decision of the Council
of Administration of the National Bank of Moldova no.227 of
18 October 2010, the licensed banks have passed to a monthly
tracking period of attracted funds and required reserves main-
tenance. Extending the term of required reserves maintenance
for a period of a month contributed to the improvement of the
operational framework of the National Bank of Moldova and to
a uniform distribution of liquidity within the banking system
during the required reserves maintenance period.
The ﬁrm management of liquidity in the banking system was
aimed at ensuring the proper functioning of the money mar-
ket, maintaining a balance between the price stability and the
national economic recovery.


Summary
7
Thus, the long-term liquidity in the banking system constituted
0.7 percent on December 31, 2011, while the current liquidity –
33.2 percent. All of the banks except B.C. „Universalbank” S.A.
have complied with both indicators. It should be mentioned
that by the Decision no.272 of November 25, 2011 on B.C.
“Universalbank” S.A. was established special administration. A
prerequisite of the implementation of inﬂation targeting regime
is to maintain a ﬂexible exchange rate. Thus, the NBM lim-
ited the interventions on the foreign exchange market to the
amounts needed, without resisting to the long-term trend of the
exchange rate of MDL.
During 2011, foreign exchange reserves rose by 14.4 percent,
up to USD 1965.3 million as of December 31, 2011, covering
3.6 months of imports.
In 2011, the NBM intervened in foreign exchange market mainly
as a buyer of foreign currency, the interventions being made to
mitigate the volatility in the exchange rate of national currency
against the U.S. dollar and providing liquidity to the foreign
exchange market.
It should be mentioned that, according to the Regulation on
Risk Weighted Capital Adequacy, since December 31, 2011
the minimum amount of capital was established at the sum
of 150.0 million lei. On December 31, 2011 all banks’ Tier I
capital corresponded to the minimum set amount, excepting
B.C. “Universalbank” S.A. The average risk weighted capital
adequacy (ratio of total regulatory capital to risk-weighted
assets) is maintained at a high level - 30.4 percent (the minimum
level in the Republic of Moldova is 12.0 percent), indicating
a high degree of safety of banks determined by the existence
of a potential of making risky operations without affecting the
capital.
The share of non-performing loans (substandard, doubtful, and
compromised) in total loans constituted 10.7 percent at Decem-
ber 31, 2011, or by 2.6 percentage points less compared to the
end of 2010.
The National Bank of Moldova reiterates that it will further
promote a prudent monetary policy, monitoring and anticipating
domestic macroeconomic and international environment, such
that, by using monetary policy instruments, to ensure price
stability over the medium term and long term economic growth.


8
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
World economy in 2011
The year 2011 was marked by numerous events in the economic,
social and political sphere as well as by natural disasters. Many
of them have contributed to changing the world economic
climate. For the world economy, 2011 was quite difﬁcult in
terms of signiﬁcant increases in food and oil prices, which
undermined the economic activity and caused pressures on
consumers’ personal budgets. The developing economies con-

tinued to experience consequences of the most severe economic
and ﬁnancial crisis after World War II expressed in terms of high
unemployment, high inﬂation rates, ﬁscal consolidation, etc.
While the world economy was recovering, in the last quarters
of 2011 new worsening signals appeared which increased the
intensity of the course of the second wave of crisis. The principal
focus of the vulnerability of the global economy remains to be
the sovereign debt problem in developed economies, manifested
mainly in the euro area countries.
Tensions in public ﬁnances in developed economies increased
signiﬁcantly, especially in the second half of 2011. Currently,
there are no identiﬁed solutions, which would provide cred-
ibility to ﬁnancial markets. Asian countries have intensively
experienced the second wave of global crisis, mainly Japan,
strongly affected by the earthquake in March 2011 and the
slowdown in world demand, as well as by the appreciation of
the yen against major freely convertible currencies.
Emerging economies have recorded a relatively high growth
rate, but this was tempered by tightening monetary policy,
aimed mainly at reducing inﬂationary pressures and anticipating
the reduction of external demand and adverse effects caused by
the propagation of international ﬁnancial instability. According
to the International Monetary Fund data, the world economy
recorded in 2011 a growth rate of 3.8 percent, which is by
1.4 percentage points less than the previous year. Advanced
economies grew by 1.6 percent, reﬂecting the halving of U.S.

developed economies in the euro area and Japan’s economic

World prices rose signiﬁcantly during 2011. Factors that pos-
itively inﬂuenced their evolution were the economic boom in
China and other Asian countries, energy prices increase on


World economy in 2011
9
average by 31.7 percent compared with 2010 due to political
tensions in the Middle East and North Africa, disturbance in
international products circuit following the earthquake in March
2011 in Japan. Also, some countries have faced additional
inﬂationary pressures generated by the depreciation of their
national currency.
Although, overall, agro meteorological conditions were quite
favorable in the main growing areas of agricultural resources,
consumption recovery led to food prices increase by an average
of 19.7 percent. In the second half of 2011, the high rates of
price growth have impelled many central banks to tighten their
monetary policy, thereby helping to reduce consumption.
Another problem in 2011 was the high level of unemployment.
Thus, the unemployment rate in the United States recorded the
average of 9.0 percent. The reduction of the unemployment rate
by 0.7 percentage points as compared to 2010 is due to the FED
policies to promote economic recovery and to keep inﬂation in
line. Thus, during 2011, The Federal Reserve kept the interest
rates benchmark at minimum levels and purchased long-term

U.S. Treasury securities, thus increasing monetary liquidity. In
the euro area, the unemployment rate has gradually increased,
so that in December 2011 Eurostat announced a record of 10.6

in countries facing sovereign debt crisis, the worst situation
being in Spain, where the unemployment rate recorded the
level of 23.1 percent in December 2011. In 2011, the average
unemployment rate in the euro area was 10.2 percent.
The evolution of international currencies during 2011 has been
subject to sovereign debt crisis in the euro area, monetary policy
decisions and to the evolution of main macroeconomic indica-
tors. In 2011, the U.S. dollar depreciated against the single
European currency on average by 4.9 percent, due to worsening
macroeconomic statistics and the problems of reaching the
maximum indebtedness of the United States. In turn, by the end

of the year, the single European currency has depreciated sig-

crisis in Greece. Japanese yen experienced a deep appreciation
following the earthquake in March 2011.
Thus, to help the country, which was strongly affected by the nat-
ural disaster, through the depreciation of the national currency
and by the resumption of exports, a monetary intervention of
several states was needed. Also, to protect the exports, Switzer-
land has decided to establish a ceiling for the exchange rate of
the Swiss franc and single European currency. Some emerging
countries have experienced strong currency depreciation as a
result of the U.S. dollar appreciation.

United States of America
U.S. real GDP grew by 1.7 percent in 2011, compared with an
increase of 3.0 percent in 2010. GDP growth mainly reﬂected
the positive contribution of private consumption and exports,
which was partly offset by negative contributions of public
expenditure and investment. Acceleration of the upward trend
of industrial production and trade during the second semester
of 2011 also contributed to the economic recovery.

Maintenance by the Federal Reserve System of reference rates
at minimum levels was consistent with social and ﬁscal policies
to counter the high level of unemployment. Thus, by the end of
2011, the unemployment rate reached the level of 8.5 percent,
which is the minimum for the last three years. At the same time,
in 2011, the inﬂation was very high as opposed to previous
years, the prices increased on average by 3.2 percent compared
with 2010.
The U.S. economy has been also affected by the problems of
reaching maximum indebtedness, which caused the deprecia-
tion of the U.S. dollar on international markets, and the down-
grading of the country rating of the U.S. On 2 August 2011, the
U.S. government has adopted the increase of the debt ceiling
and decided to cut government spending. Despite the measures
adopted, the U.S. economy continued to face low consumer
conﬁdence, which has put pressure on government to further
reduce costs in order to reduce the budget deﬁcit.
Euro zone
In 2011, the euro area economy was inﬂuenced by the chal-
lenges related to the development of sovereign debt crisis, the
high level of prices and unemployment. Greece, Spain, Italy
and Portugal were forced to adopt increased austerity mea-
sures, which reduced the pace of economic activity, especially
in constructions and industry. At the same time, countries with
competitive economies based on exports have experienced a

Source: Eurostat
considerable economic growth, for example the Baltic States,
Poland, Austria and Germany. In 2011, the euro zone GDP grew
on average by 1.4 percent compared with 2010, despite the
substantial economic slowdown in the fourth quarter of 2011.
Another factor that contributed to the slowdown of economic
growth in the euro area was the increase of monetary policy rate
by the European Central Bank in April and July of 2011. The
effect was felt most strongly in the constructions sector and real
estate market, due to the increase of interest rates of loans by
commercial banks. However, the monetary policy did not have
the desired effect on prices, which were signiﬁcantly inﬂuenced


World economy in 2011
11
by international price rises in energy and raw materials. During
2011, prices in the euro zone increased on average by 2.7
percent compared to the prices of 2010.
In the second half of the year, amid delay in the adoption of
measures to address the sovereign debt crisis and negative
macroeconomic statistics, the single European currency depre-
ciated, and the effect was felt by the peripheral economies of
Europe, like Romania, Hungary and Poland through deprecia-
tion of national currencies.
Romania
In 2011, Romania’s economy has come out of crisis. Thus,
according to the National Institute of Statistics, GDP rose by 2.5
percent compared to 2010, when it decreased by 1.3 percent.
The industry was one that contributed most to GDP growth.
Also, agriculture and constructions have come out from reces-
sion, contributing positively to the economic growth. However,
the euro zone sovereign debt crisis dampened the economic

boom in the Romanian economy. Decrease in the euro zone
private consumption contributed to the reduction of Romanian
exports in the second half of 2011, and the low volume of
remittances negatively inﬂuenced the consumption in Romania.
At the same time, Romania continues to have one of the lowest
unemployment rates in the region and the prices in December
2011 were by 3.1 percent higher compared with December
2010 - which is one of the lowest levels of inﬂation among the
periphery countries of the European Union.
Russian Federation
According to the Federal State Statistics Service, the Russian
economy rose by 4.3 percent in 2011 compared with 2010.
Economic boom was fuelled by increased energy prices on inter-
national markets. Also, there was recorded a signiﬁcant recovery
in agriculture, following the summer drought in 2010. Volume
increases were recorded in the sectors of ﬁnancial activity, health
and social assistance, compared with volume reductions of the

activity in these sectors in 2010. Education sector continued
in 2011 to record losses of volume. Also, exports registered a
slight increase of just 1.0 percent.
The abundant harvest has also contributed to the slowdown in
inﬂation up to the level of 6.1 percent in December 2011, com-
pared to December of 2010, which is the lowest level for the last
two decades. During the year, to reduce inﬂation, the Central
Bank of the Russian Federation increased twice (in February and
May) the monetary policy rate by 0.25 percentage points. The

reduction of the inﬂationary pressures encouraged consumers to
spend more, while the wages and disposable income increased.
At the end of 2011, amid worsening economic situation, the
monetary policy was relaxed, thus after the reduction of the
monetary policy rate by 0.25 percentage points at the end of
December, it reached the level of 8.0 percent.
Ukraine
According to data of State Statistics Committee of Ukraine, the
economic growth in Ukraine was 5.2 percent in 2011, which
exceeds the forecasts.
Under international ﬁnancial instability, the growth recorded
in agriculture - as a result of the rich harvest in the respective
year, the growth of industrial production and the constructions

sector recovery have contributed to the recording of a signiﬁcant
growth, which seems to bring the Ukrainian economy to the pre-
crisis 2008-2009 level. Increased economic activity and private
ﬁnal consumption stimulated exports and imports. Volume
decreases were recorded in publicly funded economic activities

During the year, at the insistence of the IMF, Ukraine has
reformed the pension system, passed a new bankruptcy law
and launched a program to reduce the budget deﬁcit. However,
Ukraine has not agreed to increase gas price for domestic con-
sumers, leading the IMF to continue blocking the credit tranches
for Ukraine. In December 2011, the prices in Ukraine were by
4.6 percent higher than in December 2010. At the same time,
in 2011, prices increased on average by 8.0 percent.


13
Chapter 1
The economic situation of the
Republic of Moldova in 2011
1.1
Real sector
Gross domestic product
According to the preliminary data presented by the National
Bureau of Statistics, in 2011 GDP increased by 6.4 percent
compared to the previous year. It should be mentioned that,
Moldova’s growth model has undergone signiﬁcant changes
in 2011. Thus, the increased external demand for domestic
products and augmented disposable income of the population
for the analyzed period constituted the basic factors that favored
the growth of GDP in 2011. At the same time, the economic
growth was supported by positive developments in all sectors.
In terms of uses (Chart 1.1), data for 2011 conﬁrm that foreign
demand is becoming increasingly important in the economic
growth model of the Republic of Moldova. Thus, the economic
recovery of Moldova’s main trade partners has stimulated an in-
crease of 28.6 percent in the annual rate of exports, generating a
maximum positive contribution to the GDP growth. At the same

time, household consumption rose by 8.5 percent, mainly due to
the increase of its ﬁnancing sources (higher wages, remittances,
social transfers, and loans) during the period, with a signiﬁcant
positive impact on GDP growth.
Similar factors, such as the recovery of expectations of economic
agents and their access to funding sources, determined the
increase of capital formation by 12.5 percent, with an important
contribution to the evolution of GDP, but less than the previous
year. Within the structure, the gross capital formation increased
mainly due to gross ﬁxed capital formation increase by 10.7
percent because, although it registered a signiﬁcant increase
(57.8 percent), the contribution of component “inventories” was
less signiﬁcant. However, the year 2011 was characterized by an
annual growth rate of imports of 19.3 percent, by 5.0 percent-
age points higher than in 2010 due to the increased domestic

the same time, the government ﬁnal consumption registered a
decrease of 1.6 percent, but its contribution to GDP was minor.

were recorded positive developments in all sectors. The largest
contribution to GDP growth came from net taxes increase on
products by 10.4 percent.Gross value added rose by 5.6 percent
in 2011, due to the acceleration in annual growth rate of sub-
components of “goods” and “services” with 6.3 percent and 5.4

At the same time, in 2011 there was recorded a more moderate
increase (7.1 percent) in industry as compared to the previous
year, due to the negative growth rates of production volume in
the energy sector (minus 4.6 percent). Unlike the previous pe-
riod, there was recorded a slight increase in the annual growth
rate (10.7 percent compared with 2010) of trading activities.
Positive contributions to GDP growth, but lower than those
mentioned above, were generated by the gross value added
growth in sectors as “other services” (3.1 percent), “agriculture”
(5.5 percent) and “transport and communications” (6.0 percent).
Similarly to the previous year, the lowest contribution to GDP
growth was driven by the developments in the construction
sector, which grew by 4.7 percent compared with 2010. It
should be mentioned that both the increase recorded in 2011
and that recorded in 2010 did not contribute to the full recovery
of this sector after the severe decline in 2009.
Consumer demand
Household consumption in 2011 recorded an annual increase of
8.5 percent, by 0.7 percentage points below the level recorded
in 2010 due to slow growth of funding sources.
The expenditure related to the procurement of goods for ﬁnal
consumption increased by 13.4 percent as compared to 2010
and has had the largest contribution to the growth in household

to that of the expenditure for goods procurement. At the same

with a value of 7.9 percent in real terms1 as compared to the
same period of the previous year. In this respect, the main
source of funding remained to be the cost of labor (wage bill),
which recorded an increase of 10.7 percent in real terms as
compared to 2010 and thus has generated the maximum posi-
tive contribution to the funding sources dynamic. Remittances,

A positive contribution (1.1 percentage points) but lower than

contribution to the funding sources growth.
Gross capital formation
Although in 2011, the gross capital formation increased by
12.5 percent, the annual rate was lower than the previous year
(18.1 percent). The above-mentioned positive development was
due to the evolution of both components: “gross ﬁxed capital
formation” and “inventories variations”.
Gross ﬁxed capital formation increased by 10.7 percent as com-

pared to 2010. The most signiﬁcant positive contributions were
generated by the growth in components, “capital repairs of

cent). Signiﬁcant increases were also registered in components
“purchasing of equipment and inventory by public institutions”
(59.1 percent) and “expenses related to the development and
procurement of programs and databases” (27.2 percent), but
their impact on the dynamics of gross ﬁxed capital formation
was lower.
Investment in ﬁxed capital
In 2011, the investments in ﬁxed capital, made by enterprises
and organizations at the expense of all sources of funding,
increased in real terms by 9.3 percent as compared to 2010.
Construction-assembly works had a share of 48.2 percent in total
investment and, compared with 2010, their volume decreased
in real terms by 0.6 percent.

remain to be the own means of economic agents and population
(64.0 percent), which increased in real terms by 19.6 percent
compared to the same period of the previous year. During the

reference period, the share of foreign investors funds (4.8 per-

January-December 2010. At the same time,10.1 percent of total
ﬁxed capital investment was made at the expense of budgetary
means (both from the state budget and from administrative-

A major share in the speciﬁc structure of ﬁxed capital investment
was focused on procurement of “equipments, machines and
transport means”, which accounted for 45.4 percent of the total
volume of acquired investments (up by 4.4 percentage points as
compared to the same period of the previous year). The share
of investments directed to the construction of „buildings and
ediﬁces” constituted 33.3 percent, decreasing by 7.7 percentage
points compared to 2010.
Within the structure of investment in ﬁxed capital by ownership,
31.0 percent were attributed to economic agents with public
ownership, the major share (69.0 percent) being attributed to
non-state economic sector of national economy, which have per-
formed about 64.5 percent of the total volume of construction-
assembly works.
Industrial production
During January-December 2011, according to the National
Bureau of Statistics data, the industrial output in all forms of
property has increased in real terms by 7.4 percent as compared
to the same period of the previous year. However, a slowdown in
annual growth of industrial production has started in September
2011, so that in December 2011 it constituted minus 3.7 percent
(Chart 1.7).

Industrial output growth, compared with 2010, was due to the
increase in the output of mining industry and manufacturing by

It should be mentioned that the most signiﬁcant increases in
manufacturing were recorded in the following industrial activi-
ties: “production of medical equipment and instruments, of mea-
suring and optical instruments” (by 42.5 percent), “manufac-

“publishers, polygraph and informative materials reproduction”

footwear” (by 16.9 percent), “paper and cardboard production”

Domestic trade
According to the National Bureau of Statistics data, the turnover
volume of enterprises, whose main activity is retail trading,
was in 2011 by 17.1 percent higher than in the previous year.
However, the services had a more moderate development; the
turnover volume of enterprises with the main activity of market
services rendered to population was by 1.2 percent higher than
in 2010.

Foreign trade
According to the National Bureau of Statistics data, during 2011,
the annual export growth rate exceeded constantly the import
growth rate, only in the last four months of the year, there was
recorded a reverse situation, with a slight recovery in the last
month of the year.
Thus, in December 2011, the annual growth rate of exports was

Source: NBS
of 21.8 percent, by 0.2 percentage points higher than that of
imports, contributing to the creation of prerequisites to temper
the annual growth rate of GDP in the fourth quarter, 2011.
It should be mentioned that in January-December 2011, com-
pared to the previous year, the exports of goods increased by
44.1 percent. The main factor contributing to this increase
was the signiﬁcant increase in exports to Russian Federation,
Romania and Italy, the shares of these countries in total exports
being the largest. However, by groups of countries, the Euro-
pean Union (49.0 percent) held the most signiﬁcant share, the
exports to this area increased by 49.2 percent, while the volume


18
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
of goods exported to CIS countries (with a share of 41.4 percent)
increased by 47.3 percent compared to January-December 2010.
Within the structure of groups of goods, the components that
have contributed signiﬁcantly to the growth of export of goods
in 2011, as compared to the previous year, were: “miscellaneous
manufactured articles” (by 9.7 percentage points), “raw inedible
materials, except fuels” (by 9.1 percentage points), “machinery
and transport equipment” (by 8.8 percentage points), “manufac-
tured goods classiﬁed mainly by raw material” (by 6.3 percent-
age points) and “food and livestock” (by 3.9 percentage points).
At the same time, there were recorded negative contributions
from the groups “sugar, products based on sugar and honey”,
“livestock” and “gas and industrial products from natural gas”,
which contributed to the decrease of exports by 1.0, 0.2 and 0.1
percentage points, respectively.
Since the beginning of the year, the imports of goods increased
by 34.7 percent compared to January - December 2010, due to
considerable increases in imports from the Russian Federation,
Ukraine and Romania, these countries holding the largest share
in total imports. By groups of goods, components that have
contributed signiﬁcantly to the growth of imports of goods in
January-December 2011, compared to the previous year, were:
“mineral fuels, lubricants and derivatives”, “machinery and
transport equipment”, “manufactured goods classiﬁed mainly by
raw material” and “chemicals and derivatives not indicated else-
where”, which contributed by 9.7, 9.1, 6.2 and 4.0 percentage
points, respectively.
It should be mentioned that the coverage rate of imports with
exports in 2011 constituted 42.8 percent, as opposed to 2010,
a period that recorded the value of 40.0 percent.
Transport of goods
According to the National Bureau of Statistics data, the enter-
prises of railway, road, river and air transport have transported
in 2011 goods by 16.8 percent compared to the previous year.
The annual growth rate of the volume of goods transported
recorded a downward trend, accentuated in the ﬁrst half of
2011, decreasing from 70.7 percent in February to (minus) 3.5
percent in May 2011.

Labor market
Work force
Indicators published by the NBS for 2011 reﬂect a stabiliza-
tion in the labor market situation in Moldova compared with
2010. Thus, the economically active population increased by
1.8 percent compared to the previous year. At the same time,
the number of people employed in this period increased by 2.6
percent compared to 2010, which is determined by the increase
of population employed in trading, agriculture and industry.

Within the structure of the active population, the employment
share was of 93.3 percent, increasing by 0.7 percentage points
compared to the previous year, which contributed to the de-
crease of unemployment on national scale to 6.7 percent.
This situation on the labor market indicates an improvement
in the process of creating new jobs in Moldova’s economy and
maintaining of existing jobs.
According to the National Employment Agency (NEA), the num-
ber of layoffs dropped signiﬁcantly compared to the previous
year. Within the distribution on the activities of the national
economy, the largest concentration of population employed is
registered in agriculture. Its share was 27.5 percent in 2011,
maintaining at the previous year level. It is noteworthy to men-
tion the decreasing development of number of people working

Wages
According to the National Bureau of Statistics, the average wage
of a worker in the national economy in 2011 amounted to
3193.9 lei, increasing in nominal terms by 11.6 percent, as
compared to 2010, and in real terms – by 3.7 percent.


20
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
The greatest increase in average real wage in 2011 was recorded
in agriculture (11.6 percent), but it is still the lowest compared
with salaries for other activities. A signiﬁcant increase in the
growth rate of real average wage was registered in commerce
- 7.8 percent compared to the previous year. Public admin-
istration, transport and communications are two areas that
have experienced the greatest reductions in average real wages
(minus 3.1 and minus 2.0 percent, respectively).

Inﬂation
Consumer price index
After an upward trend in the ﬁrst half of 2011 and a relatively
stable trend over the past four months, the annual inﬂation
rate was 7.8 percent in December 2011 as a result of both
pro-inﬂationary and disinﬂationary inﬂuence of several factors.
Thus, price increases in 2011 were caused mainly by:

• increases in tariffs for electricity, gas and heating supply
conducted in February, May and October 2011, which
determined the regulated prices growth;
• increase in international oil prices, in the ﬁrst half of 2011,
inﬂuenced by the social and political tensions in Northern
Africa and Middle East;


Chapter 1. The economic situation of the Republic of Moldova in 2011
21
• second-round effects caused by increases in tariffs for
electricity, gas and heating, fuel and food prices, which
resulted in higher prices of goods and services related to
core inﬂation;
• increase in domestic demand due to population dispos-
able income growth, along with increasing transfers of
Moldovan citizens working abroad and wages;
• increase in external demand along with the recovery of
economies of major trading partners in the ﬁrst half of
2011.
At the same time, prices growth in 2011 was tempered by the
following factors:
• appreciation of the national currency against the U.S. dol-
lar and against the currencies of the main trading partners;
• atypical weather conditions in the fourth quarter of 2011,
which prevented the seasonal increase in food prices;
• signiﬁcant increase in production of certain agricultural
crops;
• reduced food prices worldwide.
Within the components structure, the highest increase in De-
cember 2011, as compared to December 2010, was recorded in
prices for services (11.6 percent), followed by food prices (7.4
percent) and non-foodstuff prices (by 5.2 percent).
The annual core inﬂation rate2 was of 6.1 percent in December
2011. Consequently, the difference between the CPI and the
core inﬂation rate was of 1.7 percentage points in December
2011. This difference points to the signiﬁcant inﬂuence of the
non-monetary factors on the inﬂation process in the Republic of
Moldova. In this respect, the overall inﬂation rate was driven in

Core inﬂation index
The annual rate of core inﬂation3 recorded a decline in the ﬁrst
quarter of 2011, and then increased for about two quarters, up
to the value of 6.8 percent in September 2011. By the end of
2011, the annual core inﬂation rate began a downward trend,
registering 6.1 percent in December 2011 due to weakening
domestic demand and the trend of appreciation of the national

Prices increase of goods and services related to core inﬂation in
December 2011, as compared to December 2010, was caused
mainly by higher prices of construction materials - by 15.2 per-
cent, public alimentation - by 11.0 percent and footwear - by 5.1
percent. Signiﬁcant contributions, but of lower intensity, were
determined by the increases in the components of “education
and training” (by 12.8 percent), “articles of sanitation, hygiene
and cosmetics” (by 6.1 percent), “detergents” (by 5.2 percent),
“clothing” (by 2.6 percent), as compared to December 2010
(Chart 1.18).
These price increases are a consequence of the side effects as
a result of increases in tariffs for utilities, fuel and food prices
and of the rising demand from the population in the ﬁrst half of
2011, driven by higher disposable income. In the fourth quarter
of 2011, the domestic demand pressures have been mitigated
along with moderating growth in real wages and remittances,

Chapter 1. The economic situation of the Republic of Moldova in 2011
23
which resulted in a reduction of the annual inﬂation rate during
that period.
Food prices
Food prices during 2011 were characterized by a slight decrease
of the annual rate in the ﬁrst three months of the year (to
4.8 percent in March), and a later acceleration up to 13.6
percent in August.
This dynamic was due to higher prices
for “vegetables”, “potatoes”, “milk and milk products”, “bakery
and milling products”, “eggs” and “sugar” (Chart 1.19).

The main factors that led to higher prices for “potatoes” and
“vegetables” were heavy rains in June 2011, which disadvan-
taged the collection and marketing of these products and the se-
vere drought in August, the condition of maintaining vegetables
price at a high level. The upward trend in prices for eggs was
inﬂuenced by lower production by 2.0 percent in January-June
2011 compared to the same period of 2010, due to the decrease
in egg production in farms by 17.7 percent. At the same time,
signiﬁcant contribution from sugar prices to the evolution of
food prices was driven by the gap between domestic prices and
the Ukrainian prices.
It should be mentioned that the increase of fuel prices, electricity
and gas in the ﬁrst half of 2011 determined the prices increase
of the above-mentioned products. Also, food prices increase
was driven by the aggregate demand growth due to higher
disposable income of the population, as a result of increased
wages and social transfers made in early 2011 and growing
remittances. However, in the ﬁrst seven months of the reporting
period, the export of food and livestock grew by 50.1 percent
compared with the same period of 2010, which caused a signiﬁ-
cant reduction of supply on the domestic market.
The last four months of 2011 were characterized by the steady
annual growth rate reduction of food prices. This development
was favored by the reduction of the contribution to annual
growth of food prices in the group of “vegetables” and the
negative contribution of component “potatoes4”. At the same
time, relatively low prices were inﬂuenced by an oversupply of
these products recorded both on domestic and regional markets
(Ukraine, Russian Federation, Poland, Spain, Italy, Germany,
Belgium) and the atypically warm weather (in the fourth quar-
ter, 2011), which facilitated their preservation and selling. In
addition to internal factors, the reduction of the growth rate
of food prices in the fourth quarter of 2011, compared to the
previous quarter, was driven by lower annual rate of world food
4http://agravista.md/article/89048/index.html


24
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
prices, which fell from 33.9 percent in July 2011 to minus 5.5
percent in December 2011.
Regulated prices
Prices for regulated services have risen signiﬁcantly in 2011,
reaching in December values by 9.5 percent higher than in the
same month of 2010.
The regulated prices growth in 2011 was determined mainly
by price increases for gas, centralized heating and electricity in
Chart 1.20: Components contribution to the

spring and fall of 2011, decisions motivated by higher natural
gas import prices, which have stimulated further growth in
related services (Chart 1.20).
Thus, the adoption of new tariffs during 2011 resulted in in-
creased prices for natural gas, centralized heating and elec-
tricity in the CPI in December by 40.1, 42.7 and 10.3 percent
respectively. These increments, along with price increases for
water and sewer services by 6.6 percent, determined the over-
all increase of the cost of utilities services by 22.0 percent in
December 2011, compared with the same period of 2010.
At the same time, positive contributions, but of a lower intensity,
to the growth of regulated prices were driven by higher prices
for rent, health services and passenger transport rates. These, in
December 2011, were by 10.1, 4.4 and 0.4 percent, respectively,
higher than the same period of 2010.
The only negative contribution to the annual growth rate of
regulated prices was caused by price developments in medicine.
These, due to the administrative measures applied in early 2011,
recorded a decrease of 3.1 percent compared with December
2010.
Fuel prices
At the end of 2011, fuel prices were by 10.9 percent higher
compared with the similar period of 2010, which was deter-
mined mainly by price increases for petroleum products in
international markets, due to the tensions in North Africa and
Middle East.
In 2011, the average oil price5 was USD 108.9 per barrel, by
39.6 percent more than in 2010. Changes in oil prices have led
directly to changes in gasoline and diesel Platt’s prices, which,
in turn, were reﬂected directly in the import prices for these
products. Thus, in nine months of 2011, the average import
5Urals brand


Chapter 1. The economic situation of the Republic of Moldova in 2011
25
price of gasoline was USD 1033.3 per tone, increasing by 38.3
percent compared to the same period of 2010.
The average import price of diesel fuel increased by 42.6 percent
compared to the same period of 2010, constituting USD 1000.9
per tone. For January-September 2011, the average import price
of liqueﬁed gas was USD 839.9 per tone, up by 36.7 percent
compared to the same period of 20106.
The increase of fuel prices was mitigated by the tendency of
appreciation of the national currency against the U.S. dollar. In
this respect, the average rate of MDL in December 2011 was
MDL/USD 11.79, appreciating by 3.0 percent from December
2010.

Industrial production price index
In 2011, the annual rate of industrial producer prices registered
an upward dynamic, increasing from 4.2 percent in January to
8.8 percent in December 2011 (Chart 1.22). In December, this
trend has slowed down, so that the annual rate of industrial
price production index (IPPI) was 8.1 percent, decreasing by 0.7
percentage points compared to the previous month. Within the

structure, the largest increases were recorded in prices of the
energy industry and manufacturing, which grew by 14.8 and
7.1 percent respectively compared to December 2010, while
prices of extractive industry registered an annual rate of minus
1.0 percent.
In comparison with November 2011, prices in industry increased
overall by 0.4 percent. This increase was mainly inﬂuenced
by price increases in manufacturing and mining, which rose
by 0.5 and 0.2 percent, respectively. At the same time, the
energy industry has recorded a zero growth rate of prices. It
should be mentioned that the most signiﬁcant price increases
were recorded in the following manufacturing subcomponents:
“tobacco articles manufacturing” (3.1 percent), “manufacture
of medical, precision, optical instruments and apparatuses,
watch and clocks” (2.9 percent), “publishers, polygraph and
informative materials reproduction” (1.4 percent), “footwear

Construction prices
In the fourth quarter of 2011, the annual rate of price increase
in constructions constituted 9.3 percent, increasing by 0.6 per-
centage points compared to the previous quarter. The rapid
annual growth of prices in constructions in early 2011 was due
to the revival of this sector after the crisis in 2009.

1.3
Financial situation
After a year of pronounced recession, 2011 was characterized by
a more moderate pace of development of the national economy.
As a result of measures taken during the reporting period there
was an improvement in the business environment and the public
sector has been streamlined.
According to the information provided by the Ministry of Fi-

nance during January - December 2011, the dynamic of the
revenue accumulation in the budget recorded a mitigation of
the annual growth rate, up to 9.5 percent (compared to 17.1
percent in 2010), constituting 30158.5 million lei (Chart 1.24).
The ratio of collected revenue to GDP for 2011 constituted 36.7
percent or by 1.6 percentage points less than in 2010.
During the reporting year, there was further recorded the high
dependence of the budget on household consumption due to
increased share of indirect taxes in total revenues, and high
prices led to increased accumulation of funds in the national
public budget, from import duties. Tax revenues continued to
hold the major share in budget revenues, the main source of
income remained to be, as in the previous years, the domestic
taxes on goods and services (up by 16.3 percent), in particular,
income earned from VAT (up to 14.4 percent annually).
At the same time, the direct taxes contributed on average by 11.1
percent to the increase of budget revenues in January-December
2011, a signiﬁcant contribution going to the state mandatory


Chapter 1. The economic situation of the Republic of Moldova in 2011
27
social insurance (up by 10.0 percent) and income taxes (up
to 15.4 percent). As a result of these indicators increase, tax
revenues rose by 13.6 percent. During the reporting year, the
Republic of Moldova has beneﬁted from ﬁnancial assistance
for budget support in the form of external grants totaling USD
141.5 million (1661.3 million lei, by 13.2 percent below the
previous year) and internal grants totaling 43.0 million lei
(by 51.8 percent below the previous year).
The assistance
provided by the international ﬁnancial organizations and donor
countries constitute an important contribution to the economic
recovery and to the implementation of reforms needed for the
modernization and the recovery of the Republic of Moldova.
During January-December 2011, the allocation dynamic of
budget expenditures registered an upward trend, recording an
annual growth rate of 9.5 percent (from 7.2 percent in 2010),
representing 32117.0 million lei. This development was due
mainly to increased spending on insurance and social assistance
(by 8.2 percent), education (by 4.5 percent), health care (by
6.6 percent).
Within the structure of the major public expenditure, for social-
cultural programs were directed – 71.5 percent, for national
economy – 11.3 percent, for defense, public order and national
security - 5.6 percent, and for government service with general
purpose - 4.5 percent.
The ratio of public expenditure to preliminary data for GDP
constituted 39.1 percent in 2011 or by 1.7 percentage points
less than in 2010.
In the context of development of economic activity in the
Republic of Moldova, the major components of the national
public budget were characterized by expenditure increase and
slowdown in the pace of growth in total revenues.
Thus, in January-December 2011, the consolidated budget exe-
cution resulted in a deﬁcit of around 2.0 billion lei, increasing
by 9.6 percent annually. The ratio of this deﬁcit to preliminary
calculated GDP represents 2.4 percent or by 0.1 percentage
points less as compared to 2010.
On December 31, 2011, the state debt amounted to about
19226.5 million lei. The balance of external debt increased by
2.4 percent compared to January 1, 2011 and constituted USD
1142.5 million, evolution justiﬁed by the fact that the external
state loans exceeded the external state loan repayments by about
USD 34.5 million. At the same time, the negative ﬂuctuation of
the U.S. dollar exchange rate against other foreign currencies,
in the amount of USD 8.2 million, led to an attenuation of


28
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
the external debt stock increase. Internal state debt increased
by10.1 percent as compared with the beginning of the year,
representing 5841.9 million lei. The modiﬁcation of the internal
debt occurred exclusively at the expense of raising the issue of
state securities via actions by 264.5 million lei and the redemp-
tion of SS by subscription in the amount of 100.0 million lei, as
well as the issue of SS to ensure ﬁnancial stability in the amount
of 428.5 million lei and their redemption in the amount of 55.9
million lei.

of 6.4 percent in real terms, which positively inﬂuenced the
international economic relations, the exports of goods and
services increased by 37.5 percent, exceeding by more than
5.0 percentage points the growth rate of imports.

instruments – USD 4.63 million. In addition, reserve assets de-
creased by USD 35.11 million due to exchange rate ﬂuctuation
of other currencies against the U.S. dollar.

Gross external debt of the Republic of Moldova increased

totaled USD 3404.42 million (+10.5 percent) and were made
up of loans from direct foreign investors – 22.0 percent, other
long-term liabilities – 72.7 percent and SDR allocations – 5.3
percent.

35
Chapter 2
The activitity of the National
Bank of Moldova
in 2011
2.1
Achievements of the monetary and
foreign exchange policy in 2011
The fundamental objective of the National Bank of Moldova is
to ensure and maintain price stability. Without prejudice to its
primary objective, the National Bank of Moldova promotes and
maintains a ﬁnancial system based on market principles and
supports the economic policy of the state.
In order to achieve this goal, the National Bank of Moldova
establishes and maintains the monetary, credit and foreign
exchange market conditions that lead to the sustained and
stable economic development of the state, and, in particular, of
the ﬁnancial and currency system based on the market laws.
Regulatory framework of the monetary policy in 2011
During 2011, the NBM acted in accordance with the Monetary
Policy Strategy for 2010-2012, approved by the Council of
Administration of the National Bank of Moldova on December
29, 2009 and amended on December 30, 2010. In this con-
text, quantifying the macroeconomic situation, the trends and
projections of macroeconomic indicators in the medium term,
inﬂation outlook in the short and medium term, with possible
uncertainties and challenges during the reference period, as
well as to anchor inﬂationary pressures, the National Bank of
Moldova has promoted a prudent monetary policy during 2011.
This has helped the National Bank of Moldova to maintain the
conditions necessary for the continuation of the disinﬂationary
process, according to its objectives stipulated in the Monetary
Policy Strategy for 2010-2012, as a result the value of annual
inﬂation of 7.8 percent in December 2011 was lower than the
level of 8.1 percent recorded in December 2010.


36
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
Macroeconomic analysis and forecasting activity
During 2011, the monetary policy has focused on further devel-
oping and maintaining of future behaviour, anticipating macroe-
conomic developments by applying the available instruments
to achieve the inﬂation target set and to avoid any eventual
deviations.
In this respect, the NBM continued to develop and maintain
this system throughout the year. Currently, the analysis and
forecasting activity continues its applicative character, a process,
which in terms of organization takes place in the Monetary
Policy and Research Department.
The main activities in 2011 were directed towards the develop-
ment and extension of the central model for medium-term inﬂa-
tion forecast. Expanding the model implied the incorporation of
new behavioral equations into the forecasting model, the inclu-
sion of new indicators that allowed detailing the economic phe-
nomena typical for Republic of Moldova. In this respect, there
have been made a number of researches, which were based
on techniques and statistical analysis, in order to determine
and quantify the impact of behavioural relationships, based on
impulse-response functions of macroeconomic indicators. Based
on these researches and new extensions of the model were
determined and explained new segments in the propulsive chain
of monetary policy instruments on macroeconomic indicators,
particularly on the consumer price index (CPI).
Such changes have been made gradually, using, every time,
rigorous testing techniques, such as impulse-response function
analysis, making forecasts based on historical samples, historical
decomposition of the variables through the optics of the model,
etc. Note that the revaluation of parameters by re-estimation
and/or recalibration bears a continuous character. These pro-
cedures are performed for a more precise representation of
economic processes in Moldova, which are continuously subject
to both quantitative and qualitative changes that are speciﬁc
especially for developing economies and those in transition,
which include the national economy.
Another important aspect is determined by the extensive nature
of agriculture in the Republic of Moldova, causing increased
volatility of food prices due to uncertainty in agro meteoro-
logical conditions.
Although these phenomena have a low
predictability, they still shall be taken into account in drawing
up the balance of risks that could result in the deviation of
inﬂation from the baseline scenario.


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
37
The research directions in 2011 were the quantiﬁcation of
regulated prices growth impact, both as direct impact and as
secondary effects on inﬂation. Also, the impact on economic
activity was estimated, given that a negligible share of GDP
is represented by agriculture-related activities, therefore the
prices are inﬂuenced by the performance of this sector and the
aggregate demand.
In 2011, the analytical capacity has been strengthened by estab-
lishing both formal and informal consultative relations with the
institutions providing statistical data and those whose decisions
have a pronounced impact on price developments, especially on
regulated prices.
The short-term forecasting of inﬂation has been streamlined by
creating econometric models for several major subcomponents
of the CPI, which showed a homogeneous character, whose be-
haviour had a signiﬁcant impact on overall inﬂation. At the same
time, it was attempted to introduce new factors, variables in the
models used, which, according to the econometric estimates and
tests, explained the inﬂationary process in Republic of Moldova.
Also, the performance of forecasting linear regression models
with a single equation and those with simultaneous equations
has been re-evaluated. As a result, during 2011, the deviation
between forecasted values and actual CPI was minimal and was
due to objective factors.
Communication and transparency of monetary
policy
The communication role of the central bank with the public
is crucial for the successful promotion and implementation of
monetary policy. Public communication of objectives, instru-
ments, procedures, decisions and forecasts lends credibility
to the central bank, while increasing its accountability to the
public. Providing information to different markets generates a
more effective functioning. In this context, an efﬁcient commu-
nication inﬂuences the market expectations and improves the
transmission of changes in monetary policy to the real economy.
During 2011, the transparent communication activity was the
deﬁning attribute of a monetary policy consistent with the
fundamental objective of the NBM to ensure and maintain price
stability.
The strengthening of the communication process was possible
due to an increased frequency and consistency of the messages
and due to their compliance with the interests of target-groups


38
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
(media, general public, businesses, and government), to ensure
transparent and timely information related to monetary policy
decisions.
Thus, the National Bank of Moldova has published no later than
the day after each meeting of the Council of Administration
of the NBM monthly press releases informing the public about
monetary policy decisions regarding the development of the
base rate and minimum required reserves as well as the causes
and premises underlining the decision-making process.
To strengthen the communication process of the NBM, there
have been published press releases on inﬂation, the day after the
announcement of the inﬂation level calculated by the National
Bureau of Statistics, with the identiﬁcation of the determinants
of inﬂation and its insigniﬁcant deviations from the forecast
horizon during 2011.
As from 2011, the NBM started publishing the Inﬂation Report
quarterly, which is a continuation of the Monetary Policy Report
and reﬂects a detailed analysis of the set objective in the context
of potential risks to this objective, providing explanations for
the discrepancy between the actual level of inﬂation and the
expected one, in the circumstances of the internal environment’s
adaptation to the gradual adjustments in the global economy.
To ensure the general public’s proper perception of the quan-
titative value of forecast, the NBM organized quarterly press
conferences with the participation of the Governor and the me-
dia representatives, at which were identiﬁed the conditions and
prospects of the external environment and national economy
that underlined the forecasting process.
Given the inertia of inﬂation in the Republic of Moldova and
that the success of the medium term strategy is conditioned by
the anchorage of the inﬂation expectations and therefore by
the credibility of the NBM, in May 2011, the NBM published
on its ofﬁcial website the “Questionnaire on consumers and
business environment research (QCBR)”. Its purpose was to
evaluate the short-term inﬂation expectations of consumers and
business environment, which will be reﬂected in the observation
of economic trends, determination of the stages of economic
cycle, making short-term forecasts.
At the same time, according to the Law no.239-XVI of 13 Novem-
ber 2008 on transparency in decision-making and to the Regula-
tion on ensuring transparency in the decision-making process of
the National Bank of Moldova, the NBM provides dissemination
of information in real time via the institution’s website, con-
taining also the section “Public consultations”, through which


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
39
the concerned parties (central and local public authorities and
citizens) may directly participate in the decision-making process,
thus increasing the degree of responsibility of public authorities
to citizens and society.
The National Bank of Moldova will continue to improve the
communication process and transparency of its decisions so as
to ensure a predictable and integrated communication in order
to achieve the objective set in the Medium term strategy for
monetary policy (2010-2012).
In 2012 the National Bank of Moldova will develop the Medium
term monetary policy strategy (2013-2015) in order to ensure
the continuous setting of the NBM’s priority activity directions
aimed at achieving the main objective.
Implementation and promotion of the monetary
and foreign exchange policy in 2011
The NBM’s response to increased inﬂationary pressures in the
period of January - November 2011 was to progressively in-
crease the tightening of the interest rate policy, initiated in
January 2011. Thus, the National Bank of Moldova operated
three upward adjustments of the monetary policy rate, its level
was increased from 7.0 to 10.0 percent. At the same time, the
mitigation of price increases by the end of 2011 and the high
degree of uncertainty in the global economy have prompted
the central bank to loosen the monetary policy, initiating in
November 2011 a prudent trend of reduction of monetary policy
interest rate by performing two subsequent reductions, with an
amplitude of 0.5 percentage points and 1.0 percentage points
respectively (the latter being applied from January 6, 2012).
These measures taken by the NBM had an impact on the evo-

lution of interest rates on instruments with short maturity in
the money market. But, unlike 2010, when interest rates of

recorded on the market. The average interest rate on interbank
loans/deposits during 2011 ranged from one month to another
between 6.01 – 11.36 percent annually, falling usually in the
NBM rate corridor.

the year in close proximity to the base rate of the NBM, taking

Monetary policy instruments
The components of monetary policy operational framework, spe-
ciﬁc for the strategy to reduce inﬂation, have been extensively
applied in 2011. The 2011 situation, characterized by liquidity
excess in the money market and inﬂationary pressures increase
has driven the National Bank of Moldova to act as a net debtor
in relation to the banking system.
Open market operations
The National Bank of Moldova conducted open market opera-
tions according to the approved schedule published on its ofﬁcial
website for 2011, announcing only the operations regarding the
sterilization of excess liquidity in the banking sector, by selling
NBM certiﬁcates (NBC). In order to increase the effectiveness
of money market liquidity management, the NBM increased,

starting from January 2011, the maturity on NBM certiﬁcates
from one to two weeks.
In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova organized 104 auctions
to sell NBM certiﬁcates (NBC), with the announcement of the
maximum interest rate equal to the base rate, the NBM fully
accepting the participants’ offers. Since the required interest
rates were equal to the announced maximum rate at the auction,
the NBM Certiﬁcates were sold at a rate equivalent to the NBM
base rate. In 2011, the average weighted rate of sterilization
operations amounted to 8.54 percent annually compared to
6.74 percent recorded in the previous year.
The gradual restriction of excess liquidity due to the increase
from 8.0 to 11.0 percent and from 11.0 to 14.0 percent of
the required reserves ratio from funds attracted in MDL and
foreign currency (applied in early March and in August 2011),
inﬂuenced the daily balance of sterilization downside, falling
below the level of 4.0 billion lei from March and below the level
of 3.0 billion lei from September 2011, respectively.
However, the overall average balance of sterilization operations
was high and constituted 3232.9 million lei, about the level
recorded the previous year.
In the context of higher base
rate, the cost born by the NBM for liquidity excess sterilization

increased from 217.0 million lei in 2010 to 274.9 million lei in
2011.
Lending activity
In 2011, with the persistence of a high liquidity excess, the
NBM’s lending activity had a limited character (Table A.12).
During the ﬁrst four months of 2011, the National Bank of
Moldova continued allocation of credit resources to banks under
credit contracts concluded in 2009 and 2010 with the aim of
re-crediting the economic agents from the real sector of the
economy, their value being of 7.2 million lei.

The loans were granted at the base rate of the National Bank of

According to the contract terms, until August 2011, banks paid

In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova extended the reimburse-

2009 to protect the integrity of the banking system under the
Law on additional measures to ensure ﬁnancial stability no.190
of September 30, 2011 to take over the “Banca de Economii” S.A.

The credit was extended under conditions similar to the condi-

initially when the credit was granted, the repayment of the loan
shall be made quarterly in equal installments, and the deadline
was set as of December 30, 2015.
Fulﬁlling its function of lender of last resort, in November 2011,
the National Bank of Moldova granted to B.C. “Universalbank”
S.A. a credit line amounting to 17.5 million lei, with the term
of three months, in order to maintain liquidity. The credit was
given at the base rate of the National Bank of Moldova, variable
during the credit agreement, plus a margin of 3.0 percentage
points, in terms of ensuring the full amount of loan and related
interest.


42
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
During 2011, the National Bank continued to monitor the loans
granted in previous years to credit the cooperative societies for
housing construction. As a result of on time payments and of
advance payments, at the end of the reference year the balance
of these loans declined by 22.8 percent compared with the end
of 2010, amounting to 13.9 million lei.
Thus, on December 31, 2011 credit indebtedness to the National
Bank of Moldova amounted to 394.0 million lei, by 582.4 million
lei or 59.6 percent below the level recorded at the end of 2010.
This balance decrease occurred as a result of full payment of
loans granted to banks for re-crediting the economic agents from
the real sector of the economy, payment of mature installments
of the credit granted to protect the integrity of the banking
sector and of the installments of the credits granted to CSHC
(Chart 2.4, Chart 2.5).
Standing facilities
The operating regime of standing facilities (overnight credits
and deposits), established by the NBM for 2011, allowed banks
to manage efﬁciently their liquidity and offered to the NBM
more ﬂexibility in the monetary policy implementation.
Interest rates on standing facilities of the National Bank of
Moldova in 2011 were established in a symmetrical corridor,
the deviations from the monetary policy rate constituting 3.0
percentage points.
Overnight credit facility. In the context of a high liquidity excess
in the banking system during 2011, the banks have not resorted
to the overnight credit facility.
Overnight deposit facility. Against the backdrop of increased con-
trol of liquidity through sterilization operations and increased
required reserves ratio, the banks have resort less to the deposit
facility in 2011 as compared to the previous year.
The volume of overnight deposits with the NBM totaled 61409.7
million lei in 2011, which indicates an average daily balance of
245.6 million lei, decreasing by 1.9 times compared with 2010.
On December 31, 2011 the balance of overnight deposits placed
by banks with the NBM was 746.0 million lei.
During January-November 2011, the deposit facility rate rose
from 5.0 to 7.0 percent annually, and in December it was
reduced by 0.5 percentage points.
Overall, in 2011, all of
the banks placed overnight deposits with the National Bank of
Moldova. Resorting to the deposit facility with preference at
the end of required reserves maintenance periods, banks have
demonstrated a good capacity for managing their liquidity.


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
43
Required reserves
In 2011, the required reserves mechanism continued to exercise
the functions of monetary control and banking system liquidity
management.
During the year, the required reserves regime undergone sig-
niﬁcant changes in terms of increasing the tracking periods
of attracted funds and required reserves maintenance periods,
rising the required reserves ratio, and applying the 0% required
reserves ratio of attracted funds included in the calculation base,
whose maturity is greater than two years.
Since January 2011, according to the Decision of the Council
of Administration of the National Bank of Moldova no.227 of
18 October 2010, the licensed banks have passed to a monthly
tracking period of attracted funds and required reserves mainte-
nance.
Extending the term of required reserves maintenance for a
period of a month contributed to the improvement of the op-
erational framework of the National Bank of Moldova and to
a uniform distribution of liquidity within the banking system
during the required reserves maintenance period.
In order to counter the inﬂationary pressures and to improve
the monetary policy decisions’ transmission mechanism of the
National Bank, in the context of an underdeveloped capital mar-
ket and abundant liquidity in the banking system, the required
reserves ratio was increased twice in 2011 by 3.0 percentage
points each time:
• starting with the tracking period of attracted funds Febru-
ary 8 - March 7, 2011 the required reserves ratio in
Moldovan lei, non-convertible currency, and freely convert-
ible currency increased from 8.0 to 11.0 percent (Decision
of the Council of Administration of the National Bank of
Moldova no.10 of January 27, 2011);
• starting with the tracking period of attracted funds July 8
– August 7, 2011 the required reserves ratio in Moldovan
lei, non-convertible currency, and freely convertible cur-
rency increased from 11.0 to 14.0 percent (Decision of
the Council of Administration of the National Bank of
Moldova no.109 of May 26, 2011).
Within the meeting of May 26, 2011, along with the decision
to increase the required reserves ratio, the Council of Adminis-
tration of the National Bank of Moldova adopted a decision on


44
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
amending the Regulation on minimum reserves regime, which
stipulates the application of a 0% required reserves ratio of
attracted funds included in the calculation base, whose maturity
is grater than two years.
This amendment contributed to a better management of banks

own liquidity and created facilities to support the growth of

required reserves in Moldovan lei in 2011 had a pronounced
positive trend, being inﬂuenced by both the growth of attracted
funds and the increase in required reserves ratio (Chart 2.6).
The amount of required reserves in Moldovan lei, maintained by

of required reserves maintained in the last period of the previous
year.
During the year, the required reserves in foreign currencies trend
was inﬂuenced not only by the growth of attracted resources
and the ratio increase, but also by the facility granted to banks
at required reserves formation by applying the 0% required
reserves ratio of attracted funds, whose maturity is grater than
two years (Chart 2.7).

Thus, until July 2011, both the reserves in U.S. dollars and the
euro rose simultaneously, and since August 2011, because the
amounts of funds attracted by the bank with the term grater
than two years in U.S. dollars have been higher than those in
euro, the reserves in U.S. dollar fell and those in euro continued
their upward trend.
On December 31, 2011 required reserves formed and main-
tained by banks in foreign currency totaled USD 60.5 million
and EUR 89.9 million, up by 36.0 and 73.6 percent, respectively,
as compared to December 31, 2010.
Under article 17 of the Law on National Bank of Moldova no.
578-XIII of July 21, 1995, in order to reduce the cost of bank
assets, the National Bank, for the amount of required reserves
exceeding 5.0 percent of the attracted funds, pays to banks
an interest, which in 2011 amounted to 67.6 million lei. As
compared to the previous year, due to the considerable growth
of attracted funds and the augmented required reserves ratio,
the amount of interest paid to maintain the required reserves at
the National Bank of Moldova increased by 3.9 times.


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
45
2.2
The results of monetary and exchange
rate policy during 2011
The expectations regarding the national and global economic
and ﬁnancial context for 2011, as well as the intensiﬁcation of
global economic recovery, the development of international oil
prices, domestic demand growth and positive deviation of GDP
pointed out an increased risk of inﬂationary pressures during
2011.
After maintaining for nine consecutive months the interest rates
on monetary regulation instruments unchanged during 2010,
within the meeting of the Council of Administration of December
30, 2010 the NBM, taking into account the time necessary to
transmit impulses to the real economy, decided to increase the
base rate from 7.0 to 8.0 percent annually, which was applied
from January 7, 2011.
At the same time, in terms of continuously anchor the inﬂation
expectations, the NBM increased during 2011 the interest rates
on monetary regulation instruments by 1.0 percentage points
two months in a row, so that the base rate reached the level of
10.0 percent.
Within the meeting of July 28, 2011, the Council of Adminis-
tration of the NBM decided to increase the base rate applied to
the main monetary policy operations by 1.0 percentage points,
from 8.0 to 9.0 percent annually. This decision aimed at mitigat-
ing the inﬂationary expectations, due to increased household
consumption generated by the augmented disposable income
of the population from salaries, social transfers and transfers
from abroad, and by the continuous boost of the economy in
conjunction with increasing savings of the population.
The forecast of additional pro-inﬂationary pressures caused by
risk factors such as the possible adjustments of regulated prices
(including side effects), volatility in global ﬁnancial markets,
driven by European sovereign debt crisis and the dynamics of
food and fuel prices worldwide conditioned another upward
adjustment in the interest rates on monetary regulation instru-
ments. Therefore, within the meeting of August 25, 2011 the
Council of Administration of the NBM decided to increase the
base rate by 1.0 percentage points, up to the level of 10.0
percent annually.
The analysis of the inﬂationary process determinants in the
Republic of Moldova during 2011 highlighted non-monetary


46
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
factors, which are outside the realm of the NBM’s monetary
policy inﬂuence. This was conﬁrmed by the cessation in the
upward trend of the annual inﬂation rate in the fourth quarter of
2011, along with deepening sovereign debt crisis and recession
in the euro area and worldwide, affecting the global aggregate
demand, which reﬂected directly in the reducing the level of
domestic economic activity.
However, international foreign exchange markets developments,
the dynamics of oil and food prices worldwide, the trend of
appreciation of the national currency against the U.S. dollar
and the euro have helped to mitigate the inﬂationary processes.
In these conditions, in November-December 2011, the NBM
recorded a downward trend of the monetary policy rate, making
two consecutive reductions, with the amplitude of 0.5 percent-
age points and 1.0 percentage points, respectively.
The adequacy of broad monetary conditions in terms of mon-
etary policy objective has been achieved primarily through
interest rate policy and minimum required reserves mechanism,
which showed an upward trend during 2011, increasing the
minimum required reserves ratios of means attracted in MDL
and foreign currency up to 14.0 percent of the base.
The resolute management of liquidity in the banking system
was adapted to ensure proper functioning of the money market,
maintaining a balance between the price stability and national
economic recovery.
Dynamics of monetary indicators
The monetary indicators development in 2011 can be divided
into two stages. The ﬁrst stage is characterized by a more
robust and stable growth during January – May, and the second
stage was marked by the reduction of growth rate during June –
December 2011. The behavior of all monetary aggregates was
similar throughout 2011.

creased by 14.1 percent, compared to the increase of 18.3

of the M2 aggregate recorded a relative stable growth during
January – August, reaching the highest growth of 22.2 percent
in May, and from September stood on a downward track until

47
the end of the year. The components of M2 recorded positive
developments, the deposits in national currency increased by
18.7 percent and the money in circulation - by 7.5 percent. The
deposits in MDL recorded a non-uniform trend, but also showed
active growth during January-August 2011, with a reduction in
September – December 2011.
The maximum increase in 2011 of deposits in MDL was regis-
tered in August - 23.5 percent. The money in circulation went
up continuously during January – April, in April being recorded
the largest increase in this indicator (21.4 percent) in 2011.
Since May 2011, the trend of money in circulation changed its
trajectory, showing signs of stabilization in August, and then
resumed its downward trend, recording at the end of December
2011 a 7.5 percent growth.

by 10.6 percent in 2011 versus the 13.4 percent increase regis-
tered in 2010 (Chart 2.9). The slower growth rate of M3 versus
M2 money supply is explained by the dynamics of deposits
in foreign currency under the national currency appreciation
against the U.S. dollar. The deposits in foreign currency, recalcu-
lated in MDL, showed a moderate pace of growth during 2011 -
