3.5 percent, while their growth in U.S. dollars during the same

The highest growth rate of deposits in foreign currency, denom-
inated in MDL, was recorded in June 2011 – by 17.5 percent.
In the second half of 2011, the deposits in foreign currency
have changed the trend towards a downward one, due to the
deepening sovereign debt crisis in the euro area and Moldovan
lei appreciation against the euro.
The deposits in national currency recorded a more pronounced
growth in 2011 as compared to M0 – by 18.7 percent (in 2010
they increased by 21.3 percent), contributing directly to the M2
growth. The share of term deposits in total deposits in national
currency constituted 62.5 percent in December 2011, increasing
by 0.8 percentage points as compared to December 2010. This
regrouping in the structure of deposits in national currency
shows increased degree of saving by both economic agents and
households. The increase of the total volume of term deposits
with banks during the reference year was due to the increase of
the component in national currency, the volume of deposits in
foreign currency decreasing by 108.3 million lei. As a result, the
share of deposits in national currency in the structure of total
deposits increased by 4.8 percentage points from 53.5 percent
in 2010 to 58.3 percent in 2011.

The increase of the volume of term deposits in national currency
by 4051.2 million lei (by 20.7 percent) was due to the increase
of volume of term deposits of legal entities by 2613.1 million
lei (by 33.9 percent) and the volume of deposits of individuals
by 1438.1 million lei (by 12.1 percent).
During 2011, the term deposits attracted in foreign currency
had an opposite dynamic to those in national currency under
the national currency appreciation. Expressed in U.S. dollars,
they rose by 4.7 percent (USD 64.3 million). Expressed in
MDL, the volume of deposits in foreign currency decreased
by 108.3 million lei (0.6 percent), being conditioned by the
reduction of the volume of individuals’ deposits by 313.2 mil-
lion lei (2.1 percent), while the volume of deposits of legal
entities increased by 204.9 million lei (10.9 percent). With an
economic environment that shows signs of uncertainty and the
appreciation of the national currency, the share of term deposits
in national currency up to 3 months has increased, representing
34.5 percent of total term deposits in 2011 as compared to the
level of 30.9 percent recorded in the previous year.

With the base rate adjustment by the Council of Administration
of the NBM, the interest rates applied by banks in dealing with
customers have recorded very low levels, the interest rates on
loans have recorded a continuous decrease since 2010 (Chart
2.14).
The average rate on new loans granted in national currency
in 2011 was by 1.88 percentage points lower than the level
recorded in the previous year, up to the level of 14.37 percent.
This trend was consistent in both categories of customers. Thus,
the rate on loans granted to individuals decreased by 2.33
percentage points and constituted 16.68 percent and the rate

shows that the monetary policy transmission mechanism does
not work unilaterally and largely depends on the behaviour of
banks.

Unlike 2010, when the interest rates on state securities market
were stabilized on a level close to the monetary policy rate, in
2011, these have increased signiﬁcantly and have distanced
themselves from the basic rate: T-bills of 91 days yields ap-
proaching more to the upper limit of rates corridor, and ex-
ceeding it in the case of the T-bills of 182 and 364 days (Chart
2.22).
At the last SS auctions of 2011, the average interest rates had
values of 10.62, 12.24 and 12.29 percent annually, respectively,
for maturities of 91, 182 and 364 days, being higher, by 3.52,
4.79 and 4.82 percentage points, respectively, compared to the
values recorded at the end of 2010.

determined the increase of interest rates of state securities on
the secondary market, from 8.20 percent in January to 13.37
percent in October 2011. The annual average of these rates
constituted 11.33 percent, increasing by 4.36 percentage points
compared with the previous year. In the context of rising interest
rates, there has increased the interest of both the corporate and

The low volume of transactions with state securities on the
secondary market in 2011 (26.3 million lei), compared to the
previous year (81.6 million lei), amid higher purchases recorded
on the primary market, shows the interest of banks to maintain
the ﬁnancial securities in their investment portfolios until matu-
rity. Non-bank investors’ activity was directed towards a greater
number of transactions of unit value less than last year (Chart
2.25).

money market during 2011 totaled 1609.9 million lei, increasing
by 872.4 million lei as compared to the previous year.

market continued to be characterized in 2011 by a state of

In 2011, similar to the previous years, the main component in
interbank transactions turnover was represented by interbank
loans/deposits, amounting to 88.4 percent of total transactions.
Total interbank loans/deposits constituted 1423.7 million lei,
increasing by 2.2 times compared to 2010.
A large volume of interbank loans and deposits was recorded
on the minimum maturity of the market, the share of overnight
transactions representing 74.2 percent of the total.
The monthly average interest rate on interbank loans/deposits
ranged from one month to another throughout 2011, recording
values between the minimum of 6.01 percent annually (February
2011) to the maximum of 11.36 percent annually (Novem-
ber 2011), usually falling within the interest rates corridor
of the NBM. The annual average interest rate on interbank
loans/deposits constituted 8.86 percent annually, up by 2.61
percentage points compared to 2010.
The average weighted term of transactions between banks con-
stituted 11 days in 2011, less by 4 days as compared to the
previous year.

Records of securities in book-entry system with
the NBM
The National Bank, as administrator of the securities book-entry
system (BES), provides maintenance of the register of securities
holders and organizes the ﬁnal settlement of securities transac-
tions recorded in the BES. Through the book-entry system are
conducted monetary policy operations to absorb the excess of
liquidity and to provide liquidity in the banking system.
On December 31, 2011 in the BES were registered securities in
the total amount of 9053.8 million lei at nominal value, which
divided by issuers are as follows:
I. Ministry of Finance – SS in the total amount of 6139.9 million
lei, including:


56
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
• 3493.1 million lei, or 56.9 percent are SS issued via auc-
tions and subscription on the primary market;
• 372.6 million lei, or 6.1 percent are SS issued for Banca
de Economii S.A. according to the Law no.190 of 30
September 2011 on additional measures to ensure ﬁnan-
cial stability;
• 2274.2 million lei, or 37.0 percent are SS issued and
delivered to the National Bank.
II. National Bank – NBM Certiﬁcates (NBC) in the total amount
of 2913.9 million lei.
The total amount of SS recorded in the book-entry system at
nominal value is divided by holders as follows:

Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
57
• Operations on primary market (new issues) - 101008.5
million lei;
• Redemption by the Ministry of Finance of SS at maturity -

In 2011, 99.3 percent of total transfers were DvP (delivery

operations of pledging and transfers between banks and their
customers.
2.4
Activity of the National Bank of Moldova
as ﬁscal agent of the state
Government debt to the National Bank of Moldova
In accordance with the Agreement on the balance of state debt
contracted earlier from the National Bank of Moldova in 2011,
concluded between the Ministry of Finance and the National
Bank of Moldova on November 03, 2010, the National Bank
of Moldova during 2011 conducted from its own portfolio re-
issuance of state securities that reached to the maturity.
State securities had typically maturities of 91 days and were
obtained in the NBM portfolio at the average interest rates on
SS with the same maturity, sold at recent auctions of securities
placement on the primary market, which have been in the range
of 7.17-11.26 percent annually.
On December 31, 2011 the state debt to the National Bank of
Moldova, constituted entirely of state securities, totaled 2213.4
million lei (at purchase price), remaining at the level recorded
in the previous year.
The weighted average interest rate on SS held in the portfolio
as of December 31, 2011 recorded the level of 11.02 percent
annually and the average maturity of state securities in the
portfolio on the last day of the reporting period constituted 46
days.
The market value of state securities held in NBM portfolio as of
December 31, 2011 constituted 2245.6 million lei.


58
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
State deposits placed at the National Bank
of Moldova
Under the Agreement between the National Bank of Moldova
and the Ministry of Finance of December 5, 2007 and the
provisions of the Regulation on acceptance of deposits from
the Ministry of Finance, approved by the Decision no. 358
of the Council of Administration of the NBM of December 28,
2006, the Ministry of Finance has placed at the National Bank
of Moldova in 2011 deposits in MDL with terms from 2 months
to 1 year in total volume of 449.0 million lei.
Compared with 2010, total deposits with the NBM increased by
209.5 million lei in 2011.
The deposits were placed at the base rate of National Bank of
Moldova, which was variable during the deposit contract and
increased from 8.0 percent annually in January 2011 to 9.5
percent annually in December 2011.
On December 31, 2011 the balance of term deposits of the
Ministry of Finance placed with the National Bank amounted to
40.0 million lei.
The average weighted rate of outstanding deposits as of De-
cember 31, 2011 constituted 9.5 percent annually, by 3.07
percentage points less compared with the average rate recorded
at the end of 2010.
The average term of outstanding deposits constituted 306 days,
decreasing by 59 days compared to the previous year.

11Calculated as compared to December 2000

2011, mainly conditioned by the increase in required reserves

accumulated foreign currency in anticipation of this increase
and an augmented demand for foreign currency by economic

Thus, during the ﬁrst half of January, the availability in foreign
currency of the licensed banks reached its maximum level for

2011 the availability of licensed banks in foreign currency have
declined by USD 98.3 million, amounting to USD 321.6 million,
their share in total net assets in foreign currency balance was
19.6 percent, the licensed banks having sufﬁcient liquidity in
foreign currency to ensure business continuity.

the exchange rate of the national currency against the U.S.

has taken place on the background of the depreciation of the
euro against the U.S. dollar on the international market by 9.8
percent, having a negative impact on the supply of foreign cur-
rency. An additional factor that contributed to the depreciation
of national currency was the prevailing growth of net foreign
currency sales to legal entities at the expense of growth of net
foreign currency supply from individuals, compared with an

61
USD 71.8 million) within the Extended Credit Mechanism and

Credit Mechanism for budgetary support and the ﬁrst tranche
of USD 8.6 million of the credit allocated by the IDA within
the Program to Strengthen the Efﬁciency of the Social Assistance
System. At the same time, the increase of foreign currency
reserves was due by the increase of required reserves ratio
during 2011, from 8.0 up to 14.0 percent of the funds attracted
in foreign currency.
Foreign exchange market of the Republic of
Moldova. Activity of the NBM on the foreign
exchange market
The analysis of the foreign exchange market of Moldova in 2011
indicates an upward trend in transactions carried out on the
domestic foreign exchange market against MDL.
The total turnover of traded foreign currencies against MDL
in this period amounted to the equivalent of USD 12233.213
million, increasing by 26.0 percent compared to 2010 (Table
A.13). Within the structure of the total turnover, the foreign
currency purchases on the foreign exchange market against
MDL constituted USD 6080.1 million, and sales - USD 6153.1
million.
The volume of foreign exchange transactions carried out on the
domestic foreign exchange market during 2011 has registered
an upward trend, reaching in the fourth quarter of 2010 the
maximum amount of USD 3473.1 million.
13The amount does not include the purchases/sales against MDL carried
out by the NBM with the Ministry of Finance.


62
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
Domestic foreign exchange market operations were mainly
driven by legal entities, with a well-known presence on the trans-
fer market (accounting for increases in turnover by 27.0 percent)
and through foreign exchange ofﬁces of the licensed banks on
foreign exchange cash market, which turnover increased by
20.8 percent. The National Bank of Moldova transactions and
of the non-resident banks on domestic foreign exchange market
decreased during 2011.
In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova purchased foreign
currency in the amount of USD 77.7 million and sold foreign
currency in the amount of USD 31.5 million (net purchases -
USD 46.2 million), of which USD 28.0 million - swap operations,
including USD 2.0 million - buying swap operations and USD
26.0 million - selling swap operations. Swaps sales were made
in the second half of September 2011 to mitigate the temporary
demand for foreign currency on domestic foreign exchange
market.
During 2011, the U.S. dollar has maintained its dominant posi-
tion in the total turnover of foreign exchange transactions on
domestic foreign exchange market and in the total turnover of
foreign exchange transactions on transfers market, ceding dom-
inance to the euro in total turnover on cash foreign exchange
market.

Both in 2010 and in 2011, in terms of total purchases of foreign
currency on cash and transfer foreign exchange market, the
euro dominated (51.4 and 48.6 percent respectively) while in
terms of total sales, the U.S. dollar has dominated (60.1 and
60.4 percent).
As compared to 2010, the foreign currency purchases by the
licensed banks on domestic foreign exchange market increased
by USD 1172.3 million in 2011or by 26.1 percent (Table A.15).
This increment was mainly due to increased purchases of for-
eign currency from individuals by USD 526.3 million (by 22.1
percent), from legal entities - by USD 462.6 million (by 30.5
percent), from resident banks - by USD 99.0 million (by 41.5
percent) and from foreign exchange ofﬁces - by USD 0.8 million
(by 42.1 percent).
At the same time, there was recorded a reduction of foreign
currency purchases from the NBM - by USD 34.4 million (by
52.2 percent) from non-resident banks - by USD 13.0 million
(by 61.9 percent).
Thus, the purchases of foreign currency by the licensed banks on
domestic foreign exchange market in 2011 were made mostly
from individuals (51.5 percent), from legal entities (35.0 per-
cent) and resident banks (6.0 percent).

Compared with 2010, sales of foreign currency by licensed
banks on domestic foreign exchange market in 2011 increased
by USD 1173.4 million (25.7 percent), being determined mainly
by higher sales to legal entities - by USD 919.9 million (25.5
percent), to resident banks - by USD 93.2 million (38.9 percent),
to individuals - by USD 90.6 million (20.8 percent) and to non-
resident banks - by USD 2.6 million (37.1 percent).
The sale of foreign currency by licensed banks on domestic
foreign exchange market in 2011 was made mainly to legal
entities (79.0 percent), individuals (9.2 percent), resident banks
(5.8 percent) and to the NBM (1.3 percent).
The foreign currency surplus on domestic foreign exchange
market in the total amount of USD 2520.5 million, mainly
derived from individuals (USD 2385.9 million, or 94.7 percent),
purchased by banks in 2011, was sold to legal entities (USD
2546.6 million).

65
The banks’ balance sheet liabilities in foreign currency re-
corded during the reporting period an increase by the equivalent
of USD 245.4 million (from USD 1459.4 million on December

deposits of bank’s clients.
The clients’ term deposits in foreign currency held at December
31, 2011 the most signiﬁcant weight in total balance sheet
liabilities in foreign currency (45.1%). At the end of 2011, the
balance of these deposits increased by the equivalent of USD
4.5 million (from USD 764.5 million to USD 769.0 million) or
by 0.6 percent as compared to the end of 2010.
The term deposits of non-residents decreased by 19.2 percent
(from USD 24.0 million on December 31, 2010 to USD 19.4
million on December 31, 2011). During 2011, the share of term
deposits of non-residents in total term deposits of clients ranged
between 2.1 and 5.7 percent.
At the end of 2011, the share of customers’ various deposits in
foreign currency represented 23.7 percent of total balance sheet
liabilities in foreign currency, increasing in absolute values by
the equivalent of USD 81.4 million (from USD 322.7 million
as of December 31, 2010 to USD 404.1 million as of December
31, 2011), or by 25.2 percent. From these deposits, the various
deposits of non-residents increased– by 11.8 percent (from USD
14.7 million as of December 31, 2010 to USD 16.5 million as
of December 31, 2011). During 2011, the share of various
deposits of non-residents in total various deposits of clients
ranged between 3.9 – 7.6 percent.
The share of deposits of non-residents of the total clients’ de-
posits amounted to 3.1 percent (in 2010 - 3.6 percent), ranging
between 2.9 and 5.4 percent during 2011.
The main balance sheet liabilities in foreign currency of the
licensed banks (term deposits and various deposits of clients)
increased by the equivalent of USD 85.9 million (from USD
1087.2 million on December 31, 2010 to USD 1173.1 million
on December 31, 2011), or by 7.9 percent.
The share of the balance of deposits in EUR in total of clients’
deposits in foreign currency constituted 69.7 percent at the end
of the reporting period, decreasing insigniﬁcantly by 0.3 per-
centage points compared to December 31, 2010, the minimum
share in 2011 being of 68.4 percent and the maximum of 71.6
percent. The balance of clients’ deposits in EUR increased by
the equivalent of USD 57.3 million, or 7.5 percent compared to
December 31, 2010, amounting to the equivalent of USD 817.8
million at the end of 2011.

Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
67
Management of foreign exchange reserves
In accordance with Articles 5, 16 and 53 of the Law on National
Bank of Moldova no. 548-XIII of July 21, 1995, the NBM holds
and manages the foreign exchange reserves, carries out foreign
exchange transactions using foreign exchange reserve assets and
keeps them at an appropriate level to carry out the monetary
and foreign exchange policies. The role of foreign exchange
reserves is to ensure the credibility and ﬁnancial stability of the
country, to honour the country’s payments related to foreign
liabilities of the state and to deal with unforeseen exogenous
shocks.
Within the management of foreign exchange reserves process,
the NBM provides a high level of safety and a required level of
liquidity of investments. The NBM investment policy is prudent,
aiming at optimizing proﬁtability, focusing on liquidity and
safety.
An essential element of the management of foreign exchange re-
serves process is the management of foreign reserves investment
risk, which is achieved by imposing constraints and limitations
associated with investments.
The Table A.25 shows the main ﬁnancial risks related to the
management of foreign exchange reserves and methods used to
diminish them.
The world economy in 2011 was characterized by a pronounced
political climate instability, by an alarming increase of budget
deﬁcits and indebtedness of the euro zone’s peripheral coun-
tries and in particular by the restructuring of sovereign debt
of Greece, factors that led to the intensiﬁcation of investors
uncertainty on the ability of governments of these countries to
honor their payment obligations.
Under these circumstances, during the reporting year, there
were recorded successive downgrades of rating assigned by
credit agencies to several European countries such as Greece,
Ireland, Italy, Portugal, Spain and Belgium, and at the end of
the year there were established negative perspectives of further
development of ratings in a number of euro zone countries.
It should be mentioned that these reductions did not affect
foreign exchange reserves, since NBM had no exposure to these
countries as of December 31, 2011.
As regarding the U.S. market, the U.S. government managed to
avoid deepening of the crisis by adopting expansionary mone-
tary and ﬁnancial policy. However, the American economy could
not achieve the expected growth. In contrast, the U.S. economic


68
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
growth slowed down continuously in 2011, the unemployment
rate has remained high and the federal debt problem worsened.
In this context, on August 8, 2011 the Standard & Poor’s agency
downgraded the USA’s sovereign rating position from AAA to
AA +.
The global uncertainty oriented investors to other regions and
to more stable currencies such as gold and Swiss franc, which
have reached record levels in August 2011.
The global economic development trend is also reﬂected in
the evolution of proﬁtability rates (Chart 2.40), increasing
slightly in the ﬁrst quarter of 2011, followed by a decrease
in the next two quarters, leveling out towards the end of 2011.
The evolution of proﬁtability rates was also inﬂuenced by the
base rates set by the monetary authorities (Chart 2.41).

The evolution of the base rate for the euro should be mentioned,
which has been modiﬁed four times throughout the year. In
April and July 2011, the base rate increased by 0.25 percent
and to the end of the year, in November and December, the rate
was reduced by 0.25 percent, reaching the level of 1.0 percent,
a level recorded at the beginning of the year. As regarding the
CHF, the base rate was set at zero level, starting with August
3, 2011, while before that date its value was of 0.25 percent.
Other base rates had a stable trend throughout 2011.
Ofﬁcial reserve assets rose by 14.42 percent as of December
31, 2011 or by about USD 247.63 million (from USD 1717.69
million to USD 1965.32 million) as compared to December 31,
2010.

Among the main factors that inﬂuenced the growth of ofﬁcial
reserve assets can be listed: attracting grants and loans from
IMF, EU and World Bank; increasing required reserves of the
licensed banks maintained at the NBM; interventions in the
interbank foreign exchange market and income related to the
management of foreign exchange reserves.
Given the high degree of uncertainty regarding the global eco-
nomic developments, the NBM focuses on the issues of security
and liquidity of investments. The global banking system vulner-
ability requires the approach of an investment policy focused
on limiting exposure to banking entities, increasing the share of
investment in government instruments.
Within the management of foreign exchange reserves process,
the NBM invests in safe instruments used by other central
banks: placements in correspondent accounts (usually with
other central banks), term placements in foreign currency and


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
69
securities, which are classiﬁed into: supranational securities (is-
sued by supranational institutions), government (issued by the
U.S. government, EU member governments, other governmental
issuers with high credit rating), non-governmental organizations
(issued by highly rated agencies).
Some of the foreign exchange reserves are managed externally
by the World Bank. According to the Consulting and Man-
agement Investment Agreement, concluded on December 8,
2010 between the International Bank for Reconstruction and
Development (IBRD) and the National Bank of Moldova, IBRD
became the consultant and agent of the NBM for managing a
part of external assets, limited at 20.0 percent of ofﬁcial reserve

2.6
Supervision and regulation of banks’
activity
General information
On December 31, 2011 in Moldova were operating16 15 banks, li-
censed by the National Bank of Moldova, including four branches
of foreign banks and ﬁnancial groups. The total number of banks
subdivisions constituted 1256, of which 320 are branches, 928
representative ofﬁces, and 8 exchange ofﬁces. During 2011,
banks have complied their activity, statutes and regulations with
the Law no.241 of 24.09.2010 on amending and supplementing
certain legislative acts. During this conformation, through the
decisions of the management of the banks, their representative
ofﬁces were closed and their activity was taken over by the
banks branches and by their internal structural subdivisions,
located off-site the banks branches - agencies and exchange
ofﬁces. Thus, during 2011, during the conformation period, the
licensed banks opened 29 branches and 936 internal structural
subdivisions (928 agencies and 8 foreign exchange ofﬁces),
while 2 branches and 867 representative ofﬁces were closed,
as well as 8 internal structural subdivisions (6 agencies and 2
exchange ofﬁces).
The total number of staff employed in the banking system as
of December 31, 2011 was 11169, increasing during the year
with 236 persons. On average, each employee in the banking
sector had assets in the value of 4.3 million lei, which was by
0.4 million lei (10.3 percent) more than at the end of 2010.
It should be mentioned that by the Decision no. 272 of Novem-
ber 25, 2011 special administration was established on BC
“Universalbank” S.A. The Council of Administration of the Na-
tional Bank of Moldova, within its meeting on February 15,
2012, following the ﬁnding of insolvency of BC “Universalbank”
S.A. and other irregularities, decided to revoke the license for
ﬁnancial activity of B.C. „Universalbank” S.A. and to initiate
the forced liquidation process in accordance with the Law on
Financial Institutions.
16According to the balance of sheet balances of credit and deposit accounts
established in the Instruction regarding the way of preparation and presen-
tation of reports by banks on ﬁnancial activity, approved by the Decision of
the Council of administration no. 36 of August 8, 1997 (Ofﬁcial Monitor of
RM, 1997, no.64-65, 103), with subsequent amendments.

and 11.7 percent of Tier I Capital (the maximum limit – 20.0

73
percent of the total regulatory capital of banks. The amount of
top ten net debts on loans accounted for 26.2 percent of net

The ratio of banks’ investments in long-term tangible assets at
the end of 2011 to total regulatory capital amounted to 21.4
percent (the maximal limit – 50.0 percent of total regulatory
capital). Investments in long-term tangible assets and equity
interest in the capital of economic units to total regulatory
capital constituted 23.5 percent (the maximal limit – 100.0
percent of TRC).
The share of net balance sheet assets in foreign currency in
total assets and foreign currency-linked assets constituted 42.9
percent. The share of balance sheet liabilities in foreign currency
in total foreign currency-linked assets and liabilities constituted
41.9 percent. The insigniﬁcant difference of the aforementioned
shares, which constituted 1.0 percentage points, revealed that
the foreign exchange risk is minimal and does not affect consid-
erably the ﬁnancial stability of the banking system.
As compared to December 31, 2010, the share of assets in
foreign currency and foreign currency-linked assets increased by
0.2 percentage points, while the share of balance sheet liabilities
in foreign currency in total foreign currency-linked assets and
liabilities decreased by 0.1 percentage points.
Off-balance sheet liabilities (conditional credit commitments)
equaled to 6022.8 million lei, or 12.6 percent of total assets.
Compared to the end of 2010, off-balance sheet liabilities in-
creased by 2198.1 million lei, or by 57.5 percent, and their
share in total assets increased by 3.5 percentage points. Within
the structure of off-balance sheet liabilities, the largest share
was held by the sales on current operations - 2549.2 million lei
(42.3 percent) and commitments to grant loans - 1631.7 million
lei (27.1 percent).
Capital of banks
Tier I Capital represents the part of total regulatory capital for
which the minimum required amount for performing ﬁnancial
activities is established in accordance with Article 26 of the Law
on Financial Institutions.
Tier I Capital reached the level of 7557.5 million lei in 2011
and increased by 830.6 million lei (12.3 percent) as compared
to 2010.


74
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
Tier I capital increase was due to the revenue gained during
2011 and due to the issuance of shares by four banks in the
amount of 147.0 million lei at the expense of additional funds
contributions of the underwriters of shares. Simultaneously,
one of these four banks has issued shares at the expense of
subordinated debts amounting to 64.8 million lei. At the same
time, two banks issued shares at the expense of undistributed
proﬁt amounting to 479.6 million lei.
It should be mentioned that, according to the Regulation on
Risk Weighted Capital Adequacy, since December 31, 2011 the
amount of capital is established at the amount of 150.0 million
lei. On December 31, 2011 all banks’ Tier I capital corresponded
to the minimum set amount, excepting BC “Universalbank” S.A.
The total regulatory capital includes Tier I Capital and Tier II
Capital (Tier II Capital is limited to the maximum of 100.0 %
of Tier I Capital) minus the participation shares in other banks
capital holding the license of the National Bank of Moldova
(Chart 2.51).

2.7
Payment system
According to the Law on National Bank of Moldova no.548-XIII of
21.07.1995, the National Bank of Moldova has the attribution
to supervise the payment system of the Republic of Moldova
and to facilitate the efﬁcient functioning of the payment system.
During 2011, the NBM has ensured efﬁcient and stable op-
eration of the automated interbank payment system (AIPS)
and supervised the payment and settlement systems, and the
cashless payment instruments in accordance with the Payment
System Oversight Policy in the Republic of Moldova, approved
by the Decision of the Council of Administration no.143 of
30.06.2011.
Developments in payment systems
As regarding the automated interbank payment system, the vol-
ume of payments processed in 2011 increased by 3.0 percent
compared to the previous year and exceeded 13.0 million pay-
ments and their value amounted to 537.0 billion lei.
17Return on assets = annualized net income / average assets.
18Return on equity = annualized net income / average equity.
19Net interest margin = annualized net interest / average interest-bearing
assets.


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
79
It should be mentioned that, within the payments structure, the
budgetary credit transfers held the largest share (58.4 percent
of total payments), followed by regular transfers of clients (40.8
percent). In terms of value, the payment structure has modiﬁed,
the value of regular transfers of clients (22.7 percent) exceeded
the budgetary credit transfers (16.4 percent), but the transfers
of banks in their own name and those of the NBM held the
largest share (60.5 percent of total payments).
The distribution of number of payments between the AIPS
components in 2011 represented 8.0 percent of payments and
92.0 percent of the payments processed in the real time gross
settlement system (RTGS system) and vice versa in the designed-
time net settlement system (DNS). This is explained by the
destination of the two systems: the RTGS system processes
urgent payments and those of great value, and the DNS system
– those of lower value.
The number of bankcards in circulation until the end of 2011 has
increased by 11.5 percent (911 thousands cards) as compared
to the previous year.
The value of transactions with cards issued by the licensed banks
from Moldova exceeded MDL 18.8 billion lei, increasing by 21.2
percent compared with 2010. In particular, cash withdrawal
transactions have been made (87.2 percent of total number of
operations and 92.5 percent of the total amount of transactions
made with cards issued by the licensed banks from Moldova).
Preference for using the card to withdraw cash in 2011 was
seen at its use in the Republic of Moldova (90.1 percent of total
number and 96.5 percent of the total amount of transaction
carried out in the Republic of Moldova).
In the foreign banks network, the cards issued by the banks
from the Republic of Moldova were primarily used for cashless
payments (83.2 percent of total number and 71.5 percent of the
total amount of transactions carried out abroad).
Holders of cards issued abroad during 2011 have carried out
over 960000 transactions in the network of banks in the Re-
public of Moldova in the amount of MDL 1.7 billion (up by
20.1 percent of number and 20.7 percent of value compared to
2010).
Regulation of payment systems
Following the current trends of modern payment instruments
expansion and the need to promote their safety and harmoniza-
tion of national legislation with the EU legislation, the draft
of the Law on Payment Services and Electronic Money has been


80
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
worked out and approved in ﬁrst reading by the Parliament on
December 27, 2011. This project contains provisions related to
the provision of payment services and electronic money issuance
as well as related to the protection of consumers of payment
services offered both by banking and non-banking providers.
In order to extend the scope of supervision on international
money transfers made through the SWIFT network, and to
facilitate their performance was developed and approved the
Concept for optimizing the international transfers and the applica-
ble supervisory framework, which involves the implementation
of SWIFT FINInform service and IBAN code (International Bank
Account Number).
In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova activities related to the
regulation of payment systems involved also the improvement of
the existing regulatory framework. Thus, to ensure compliance
with the requirements of combating money laundering and
terrorism ﬁnancing, and to optimize the regulations related
to the payment instruments, the Regulation on bankcards and
Regulation on credit transfer have been amended and completed.
Cooperation in the ﬁeld of payment systems
During 2011, the National Bank has closely cooperated with the
representatives of card payment systems present on Moldovan
market. Thus, in order to promote cashless payments, present-
ing the features of new products, increasing ﬁnancial education
of bankcard users, the representatives of international payment
card systems (Visa International and MasterCard Worldwide)
organized seminars with the participation of representatives
from banks, the Association of Banks of Moldova and of state
institutions.
In addition, the National Bank of Moldova worked closely with
the World Bank during 2011 within the initiative to modernize
the payment system in the Republic of Moldova, which inter
alia included the ﬁnalization of the legal framework for modern
payment services, the increase of consumer protection and
increase of ﬁnancial literacy population, creating the framework
for cooperation in monitoring and promoting cashless payments.
2.8
Information technology
During 2011, following the fulﬁllment of the Strategy for IT
development within the NBM for 2011-2015, the Information
Technology Department (ITD) has been involved in several large


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
81
projects, with an considerable impact on the NBM development.
Among the most important projects can be mentioned the imple-
mentation of a new reporting system in XBRL format by the NBM
(SIRBNM), implementation of electronic reporting for exchange
ofﬁces, development of electronic document ﬂow circuit and
the fundamental review of the normative and methodological
framework for planning and ensuring the continuity of the
activity and crisis reactions.
The SIRBNM project implementation was a challenge for the
NBM because of the complex objectives, which were focused
on optimization of reporting and information ﬂows to the NBM,
increasing the quality of reported data, including the relevance,
integrity, timeliness, accuracy, accessibility, comparability, and
consistency, reducing the costs and efforts required for com-
pliance from reporting entities, streamlining the data analysis
process, streamlining the reporting regulation framework of
the banks from the Republic of Moldova and, last but not least,
aligning to the reporting requirements applied in the EU.
In order to achieve the set objectives, a modern software solu-
tion was selected that provides a suitable support for reporting
models based on XBRL (eXtensible Business Reporting Language
– www.xbrl.org). The European practice in ﬁnancial reporting
to the ECB, as well as to other central banks, is based on this
speciﬁc reporting format, and it is considered to be successful
and of long-term, given that it ensures a high level of stan-
dardization, interoperability, comparability and quality of data
reported.
It should be also mentioned that the implemented solution
provides improved accessibility for reporting entities, given that
it offers rhree different ways for reporting: by ﬁlling in the
forms on the reporting portal, by ﬁlling in the reports in Excel
format and uploading them to be converted into XBRL format
and, respectively, by sending automatically the reports in XBRL
directly from the information system of the reporting entity
(B2B Web Services).
In order to process and analyze the data, there was implemented
an extremely powerful and ﬂexible analytical tool, which can
provide formation of dynamic reports and analyzes required
in an intuitive way for any user. The synergy of these features
allowed a radical optimization of the whole cycle of the report-
ing process, which currently can guarantee an interval of a few
days / weeks for obtaining the report from the appearance of
the necessity, which was achieved earlier within intervals of a
few months.
An important achievement refers to the change of reporting
process of the exchange ofﬁces to the NBM, by removing the old


82
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
procedures, by phone and on paper, replacing them with a tool
based on Web technologies, accessible and easy to use, allowing
to transmit reports electronically. Thus, the introduction of the
electronic reporting for exchange ofﬁces resulted in streamlining
the reporting process of the reporting entities (foreign exchange
entities) and of the NBM by reducing the efforts of introduction
and aggregation of data, by minimizing the human factor in
data collection process and by reducing the risks speciﬁc to the
obsolete reporting process.
Another foreground direction of development for ITD was the
extension of the functionality of the electronic document man-
agement system by implementing new electronic ﬂows in the
NBM documents circuit. At the end of 2011, there were im-
plemented about 44 electronic ﬂows (processes) within the
system. It should be mentioned that the development of this
system ensures the streamlining of activity processes in terms
of the ﬂuidity of informational ﬂows, automation capabilities,
control and monitoring of business processes, reduction of time
and paper resources, increased capacity of communication /
dissemination and processing of information, etc.
The continuity of the activity of the NBM represents a critical fac-
tor for successful achievement of the NBM objects and mission.
For these reasons, the revision draft of the methodological and
regulatory framework for planning and ensuring the continuity
of the NBM activities targeted a fundamental change in these
processes and in the reaction to emergencies by bringing the
best standards and practices in the ﬁeld (e.g. BS 25999).
By this alignment was sought to strengthen the NBM capacities
to react in case of incidents of continuity, to establish a unique
approach in terms of planning and ensuring the continuity of
the activities, to establish processes appropriately controlled,
optimized and documented by the management of the activities
continuity, etc.
All these processes have been consolidated
within a System for activity continuity management of the
NBM, which will ensure that the objectives will be achieved
through a structured, systematic approach and oriented towards
continuous processes.
It should be also mentioned that the ITD has undergone a
structural reorganization in 2011 in order to strengthen its IT
governance, IT project management, quality assurance, etc.

Cash operations
As of December 31, 2011 cash in circulation amounted to
12019055175 lei 72 bani, including banknotes – 11940170501

83
lei, and coins – 78884674 lei 72 bani, increasing by 7.1 percent

During 2011, the National Bank of Moldova placed cash in cir-
culation in the amount of 3553517560 lei, increasing by 16.68
percent as compared to the previous year, of which banknotes in
an amount of 3546503700 lei, and coins – 7013860 lei. At the
same time, the National Bank of Moldova withdrew cash from
circulation in the amount of 275495442 lei 50 bani. In 2011,
the National Bank of Moldova issued eight commemorative and
jubilee coins, as payment means and for numismatic purpose.
To commemorate the 20th anniversary of the independence of
the Republic of Moldova, 575 years since the ﬁrst documented
mention of Chi¸sin˘au city and the 20th anniversary of the foun-
dation of the National Bank of Moldova there was launched a
new series of coins “Historical events”.
The series of “Personalities” coins was completed with a silver
commemorative coin dedicated to 180th anniversary of the birth
of Alexandru Bernardazzi, who played an important role in the
development of architecture in Bessarabia.
Two coins minted in gold and silver designated to Bogdan Pet-
riceicu Ha¸sdeu completed the series of coins “Alley of Classics”
from “Stefan cel Mare” Public Garden of Chi¸sin˘au municipality.
Writer, historian, linguist, folklorist, politician, publicist, scholar
of European fame, Bogdan Petriceicu Ha¸sdeu (1836-1907) is
one of the most remarkable personalities of Romanian culture
of all times.
In order to promote folk traditions about the winter holidays,
dating from ancient times, there has been minted into silver the
coin “Carols”, with a face value of 50.0 lei, which complements
the series of coins “Holidays, culture, traditions of Moldova”.
The commemorative coin “Spoon-bill”, minted into silver, was
issued to represent the native fauna of our country and a species
of endangered birds. This coin completed the series of coins
“Red Book of the Republic of Moldova”, which was launched in
2011 and includes so far 9 other coins.
During 1996 – 2011, the National Bank of Moldova issued 78
jubilee and commemorative coins dedicated to various topics,
with different face values.
