Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
2.10
International Cooperation of the
Republic of Moldova
International Monetary Fund (IMF)
The Republic of Moldova is a member of the International
Monetary Fund from August 12, 1992. At the end of 2011, the
share subscription of the Republic of Moldova at IMF constituted
Special Drawing Rights (SDR) 123.2 million (about USD 189.7
million). In the context of the 14th General Review of Quotas
and Reform of the IMF Executive Board, the Board of Governors
of the IMF approved to increase the IMF capital and realignment
of quota shares of member-countries by the Resolution no.66-2
on the 14th General Review of Quotas and the Reform of the IMF
Executive Board of December 15, 2010. Thus, the Republic of
Moldova is to increase its quota subscription to the IMF from
the current level of SDR 123.2 million to SDR 172.5 million. As
regarding the IMF Executive Board, it is planned to modify the
appointment of senior executives in the IMF Executive Board.
In 2011, the payments related to the servicing of loans granted
by the IMF constituted USD 7.7 million (equivalent of SDR 4.8
million), of which principal payments related to these loans
constituted USD 6.2 million (equivalent of SDR 3.9 million) and
interest and commissions related payments – USD 1.5 million
(equivalent of SDR 0.9 million).
The NBM relations with the IMF take place in the context of IMF
missions of evaluating the performance in terms of achieving
the commitments under the IMF program supported by the
Extended Fund Facility / Extended Credit Facility (EFF/ ECF),
and annual bilateral consultations under Article IV of the IMF.
On January 14, 2010 the Moldovan authorities have signed
with the IMF a Memorandum on economic and ﬁnancial policies
for 2010 – 2012. The ﬁnancial assistance obtained from the
IMF was expected to be received starting with 2010 in equal
amounts according to each above-mentioned facility, the total
amount being the equivalent of SDR 369.6 million (about USD
574.0 million). From the total amount of ﬁnancial assistance
allocated under the IMF program, the National Bank of Moldova
was expected to disburse the amount of SDR 274.6 million, and
the Ministry of Finance - SDR 95.0 million.
In 2011, the program approved in January 2010 by the IMF
Executive Board has continued to be carried out and the NBM –


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
85
IMF relations were held in the context of three evaluations of
the program (2nd, 3rd, and 4th review), supported by ECF and
EFF funding mechanisms, the evaluations being made during
the work visits of IMF missions.
During these visits there have been analyzed the economic
developments and the progress in implementing the program,
the macroeconomic and structural policies to sustain macroeco-
nomic stability and promoting sustainable economic growth in
the future and there have been discussed the structural reforms
aimed at strengthening ﬁnancial stability, increasing public
sector efﬁciency, including tax collection, improving business
environment and liquidation of foreign trade barriers, and at
strengthening the social assistance program.
The IMF representatives have noted that the ﬁnancial sector has
improved during 2011. The banks have reported higher proﬁts
and the share of non-performing loans went down. It was noted
that the completion of work on the framework of preparation
for crisis and debt settlement framework will enhance ﬁnancial
stability and will help promote ﬁnancial intermediation activity.
Following the completion of the three reviews of the program
concluded by the Republic of Moldova with the IMF, in 2011
the Moldovan authorities have obtained immediate access to
the equivalent of SDR 100 million (about USD 159.3 million) to
strengthen the foreign exchange reserves. At the same time, as
a result of completing the fourth review, the IMF representatives
noted on consideration of granting the Republic of Moldova in
early 2012 a tranche of SDR 50.0 million.
Thus, during 2011, the National Bank of Moldova has received
from the IMF tranches totaling SDR 45.0 million (USD 71.8
million) under the Extended Credit Mechanism and the amount
of SDR 40.0 million (USD 63.6 million) under the Extended
Financial Mechanism. At the same time, the Ministry of Finance
received the credit allocated by the IMF for budget support
amounting to SDR 15.0 million (USD 23.9 million) under the
Extended Credit Mechanism.
European Union (EU)
The ofﬁcial negotiations on the Moldova - EU Association Agree-
ment have begun on January 12, 2010. The new document
will replace the current legal framework based on Partnership
and Cooperation Agreement of November 28, 1994 (in force
for the Republic of Moldova since July 1, 1998 concluded for
an initial period of 10 years, with automatic annual update)
and will include chapters from the political dialogue, economic
cooperation, trade, justice, ﬁnancial cooperation and other
cooperation policies.


86
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
The Deep and Comprehensive Free Trade Agreement between
the Republic of Moldova and the European Union (DCFTA) is
a part of the Association Agreement and brings further eco-
nomic beneﬁts for the Republic of Moldova. Unlike the current
Moldova - EU trade regime, which is a unilateral system, granted
by EU for a limited period, DCFTA will be a multilateral agree-
ment concluded for an unlimited period, providing beneﬁts
to export more than the current trade regime and long-term
predictability for businesses. The implementation of DCFTA will
result in access to the EU common market, a favorable climate
for foreign investors, optimization of institutional and legislative
framework, a real competitiveness of economic sectors, etc.
It should be noted that, following the key and additional recom-
mendations presented by the European Commission on October
26, 2010, which are focused on areas of national economy on
for approximation and adjustment to the community acquis,
on December 14, 2010 by Government Decision no.1125 the
Action Plan of the Republic of Moldova on the implementation
of European Commission for the application of DCFTA has been
approved.
By the Ministry of Economy Order no.25 of 23
February 2011 regarding the establishment of Working Group
number III “Economic, sectorial and ﬁnancial cooperation” for
the negotiation of the Association Agreement and the Deep and
Comprehensive Free Trade Agreement between the Republic of
Moldova and the European Union (DCFTA) was created a working
group responsible for technical coordination of DCFTA negotia-
tions. The working group “Financial services and competitive
environment” includes also representatives of the National Bank
of Moldova.
During 2011, the authorities continued to implement some
of the recommendations made by the European Commission,
submitting to the European Commission progress reports on
implementation of recommendations set to launch DCFTA ne-
gotiations. In this context, the National Bank of Moldova has
contributed to the implementation of the European Commission
recommendations during 2011 as fulﬁlling the commitments
assumed under the Action Plan in accordance with established
terms. As a result, the EU Council approved a mandate to open
the negotiations with the Republic of Moldova on the creation
of DCFTA, and the ofﬁcial launch date of the negotiations on
the implementation of DCFTA has been set at the end of 2011
and namely for the beginning of 2012.
According to the Partnership and Cooperation Agreement, the
institutionalized dialogue between the Republic of Moldova
and the EU is also achieved through the Moldova - EU sub-
committees, representing sectorial expert meetings that are
organized regularly. In 2011, the NBM representatives attended


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
87
the subcommittee meetings “Economics, Finance, Statistics” and
“Trade and Investment”. During the meetings a number of issues
were discussed, related to the cooperation between the Republic
of Moldova and the European Union, the country’s economic
development in various sectors, including issues related to bank-
ing sector development. European partners have appreciated
the actions taken during the reference period by the National
Bank of Moldova within the European integration process and
conﬁrmed their willingness to support further efforts to ensure
a stable and viable banking system.
During 2011, the Republic of Moldova has received ﬁnancial
assistance from the European Union in various ﬁelds.
Thus, in May 2011 was disbursed the third and ﬁnal tranche
of EUR 13.6 million within the Financial Support Program for
Sectorial Policies - Medicine, from the total amount of EUR 42.0
million. The ﬁrst two installments totaling EUR 15.0 million
and EUR 13.4 million, respectively, within the above-mentioned
program, have been received by the Republic of Moldova in
April 2009 and April 2010.
In September 2011, has been received the second installment
amounting to EUR 20.0 million from the grant given by the
signature on 16 December 2010 in Brussels of the Memorandum
of Agreement between Moldova and the European Union on Macro-
ﬁnancial Assistance in the form of grant amounting to EUR 90.0
million. The grant is expected to be received by the Republic
of Moldova in three installments; the ﬁrst tranche totaled EUR
40.0 million, being disbursed at the end of December 2010. The
disbursement of tranches is conditioned by the fulﬁllment of
commitments undertaken by the Republic of Moldova in this
memorandum, including those of the competence of the NBM.
During the year, the NBM presented regular progress reports for
achieving the commitments and a series of statistics within the
competence of the NBM.
In December 2011, the amount of EUR 14.1 million has been
received, related to the second installment of the grant provided
to the Republic of Moldova by the European Commission for
Support Program - Economic Stimulation in Rural Areas, under
the agreement signed on November 26, 2010.
Also, in December 2011, the ﬁrst tranche of EUR 13.0 million
was disbursed within the Financing Agreement on Budgetary sup-
port program to ﬁnance energy reform in the total amount of EUR
42.6 million. The program aims to support the implementation
of actions stipulated in the Energy Strategy of the Republic of
Moldova until 2020.


88
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
Group of Banking Supervisors from Central and Eastern
Europe (BSCEE)
In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova continued to work
with the Group of Banking Supervisors from Central and East-
ern Europe. Considering that, the aim of the Group, among
others, includes the mutual support in banking supervision
and assistance in its effective implementation, an exchange of
information related to banking supervision took place in 2011.
In 2011, following the request of the Central Bank of Armenia,
the National Bank of Moldova agreed to its admission as a
member of the BSCEE.
External technical assistance and cooperation with interna-
tional organizations and other central banks
In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova has worked closely with
supervisory authorities of other countries to exchange relevant
information on authorization and supervision of banks’ activity
(conﬁrming banks managers, knowing the banks’ owners). The
NBM has also collaborated with a number of international orga-
nizations such as the World Bank, the International Monetary
Fund, the U.S. Treasury Department, the BSCEE, etc. in order
to develop the national banking system.
The recent crises have shown that in the current banking en-
vironment, the challenges of new complex risks require an
integrated response. Thus, in order to manage the crisis, the
NBM has received technical assistance in 2011 from the World
Bank. Due to the technical assistance received, the NBM has
initiated the creation and implementation of a new stress-testing
model to adapt to current macroeconomic conditions. In April
2011, the NBM received technical assistance from the IMF for
developing the legal framework in preparation for the crisis. As
a result, the NBM became the signatory of the Memorandum of
agreement on ﬁnancial stability.
The receipt by the NBM of technical assistance to improve the
legal framework related to the banks shareholders constituted
another important measure in terms of maintaining stability in
the banking sector. With the support of the European Bank for
Reconstruction and Development, the NBM has received techni-
cal assistance from KPMG, Czech Republic, related to the eval-
uation of the NBM regulations pertaining to the requirements
for shareholders and their substantial shares in banks capital,
comparison of the existing procedures with EU legislation, with
similar regulations used by central banks from EU countries
and USA. In December 2011, the IMF mission made a work
visit in the NBM in the context of examining and improving


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
89
the legal framework related to the banks shareholders, the
ultimate goal being to raise transparency in the banking system
by amending the legislation referring to bank shareholders. In
the same context, it should be mentioned that the employees of
the NBM made in 2011 a study visit to the public authorities of
Poland with attributions in the ﬁeld, highlighting the experience
of Poland in reducing irregularities in transactions with banks
shares.
Adoption of International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS)
should be seen as a crucial reform for the country, as well as
for the domestic banking sector, and a key step in EU integra-
tion. The use of IFRS increases the possibility of making a real
assessment of the situation of the bank and allows interpreting
and analyzing banks ﬁnancial reports in an accessible way. Also,
the National Bank’s ﬁnancial reports can be compared with
other central banks reports that apply IFRS. In this context, the
U.S. Treasury Department provided assistance to the NBM in
2011 in areas such as training the employees with regard to IFRS
implementation, developing guidelines on the bank intervention,
techniques for on-site inspections, changes to the manual of on-
site inspections, special banking supervision and assessment of
non-performing loans, determining the systemically important
ﬁnancial institutions and early warning system.
The NBM, as any modern entity, must ensure a level of efﬁciency
and effectiveness. A strategic plan developed in the NBM will
result in strengthening its role in the society by performing
all the tasks undertaken, ensuring cost effectiveness and min-
imizing risks.
In this context, the NBM received technical
assistance during 2011 from the Netherlands Central Bank,
Central Bank of Germany (Deutsche Bundesbank) related to
strategic planning, budgeting and control process in a central
bank, being presented work methods, which subsequently will
be used in developing the NBM strategic plan. In the context
of developing the strategic plan of the NBM, Central Bank of
Germany (Deutsche Bundesbank), through its delegated expert,
organized a seminar and a workshop during which, the experi-
ence of Germany related to the strategic planning and budgeting
process has been related.
During 2011, the NBM received valuable assistance related to re-
porting and statistical processes, and the banks employees have
taken part in various events in this ﬁeld. Thus, in November
2011, there has been made a working visit to the Netherlands
Central Bank with the purpose of familiarization with organi-
zational processes related to reporting and statistics, collecting
reports from the reporting entities, analysis and preparation of
analytical materials and the dissemination of reports worked
out within the bank.


90
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
In 2011, the IMF Statistics Department has identiﬁed the NBM
as pilot institution to test the new algorithms to generate sum-
maries and reporting forms for international accounts statistics
according to the BPM6 standard, which is related to the balance
of payments ﬁeld. Upon the IMF request, the methodology of
recording exceptional transactions has been tested. In October
2011, the NBM staff has participated in national coordinators
meeting SDDS (Special Data Dissemination Standard) on data
quality and reporting extension under the SDDS-Plus, organized
by the IMF in the Czech Republic.
In March 2011, at the invitation of the Central Bank of the
Russian Federation, the NBM representatives attended a round
table to specify the methodology for compiling the statistics
relating to international personal transfers, bilateral veriﬁcation
of data, and the assessment of prospects for developing new
methodological standards in accordance with the recommenda-
tions of the International Transactions in Remittances manual.
It should be noted that in 2011, the NBM participated in a
project supported by the International Organization for Migra-
tion, to discuss with the ofﬁcials from the Russian Federation
(including representatives of the Central Bank of the Russian
Federation) issues related to the migration phenomenon and its
regulatory framework.
The NBM has participated monthly in working groups of the
regional project for the CSI states MIRPAL (Migration and Re-
mittance Peer-Assisted Learning Network), supported by the
World Bank, where issues related to international economic
migration have been discussed.
The NBM received during 2011 technical assistance in mone-
tary policy. German Economic Team of Moldova has granted
consultancy to familiarize with the techniques used by GET-
Moldova for estimating and analyzing the relevant indicators
of the Moldovan economy. At the same time, in April 2011,
the NBM received technical assistance in monetary and ﬁnan-
cial statistics from the IMF through a delegated expert, who
contributed essentially to the veriﬁcation and improvement of
monetary statistics reports, completion of 1SR, 2SR reports, as
well as to the extension of institutional coverage of monetary
and ﬁnancial statistics regarding the inclusion of other ﬁnancial
institutions.
According to its basic functions, the NBM holds and manages
foreign exchange reserves of the state. In order to manage
effectively the money supply, starting with February 2011, the
NBM participates in the RAMP program (Reserves Advisory
and Management Program) provided by the World Bank. The


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
91
program is unique because of the advice provided to partici-
pating institutions by organizing thematic seminars and on-site
individual consultations. RAMP participation includes installa-
tion and use of specialized software applications developed by
the World Bank. It should be mentioned that currently there
are 33 central banks and 11 ﬁnancial institutions participating
in this program, which allocated about USD 90.0 billion in
the World Bank for external management. Central banks of
Romania, Turkey, Ukraine, Georgia, and Russia beneﬁt now
from services provided within RAMP. An important aspect is
that the World Bank provides technical assistance to experts,
whose activity pertains to the management of foreign exchange
reserves, namely in accounting, information technology, legal
issues and internal audit. During 2011, the World Bank had four
missions in the NBM, during which was given advice in areas
related to foreign exchange reserves management: information
technology, accounting and reporting issues, strategic asset
allocation, investment portfolio index, etc.
During 2011, the National Bank worked closely with the World
Bank within the project “Modernization of payments system”.
The project aims to support the development of a comprehensive
strategy for modernizing the payment system of the Republic
of Moldova, to improve the safety level and efﬁciency of the
payment systems in the Republic of Moldova and to promote
public conﬁdence in the safety and efﬁciency of payment in-
struments and, therefore, in the ﬁnancial sector and ﬁnancial
institutions. During August-September 2011, the remittances
market of the Republic of Moldova has been evaluated by
the World Bank and during September-November 2011, the
draft law on payment services and electronic money, issued
by the National Bank of Moldova, has been evaluated, which
transposes the provisions of EU Directives: 2007/64/EC on
payment services in the internal market and 2009/110/EC
on the taking up, pursuit and prudential supervision of the
business of electronic money institutions. Also, a mission for
the assessment of oversight framework of the payment and
settlement systems has taken place in November 2011 and a
mission for the assessment of retail electronic payments market
in the Republic of Moldova has taken place in December 2011,
in order to ensure ﬁnancial protection for consumers and their
ﬁnancial education development.
In order to exchange experience in IT governance, in September
2011, there was made a working visit to the National Bank of
Poland. During the visit, there were discussed issues related to
organizing IT function, IT processes, the NBP’s achievements
in implementing software solutions (e.g. in the organization
and development of IT infrastructure, risk management systems,
reporting systems, etc.). As a result of the technical assistance,


92
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
the NBM managed to assimilate certain practices taken from the
experience of the NBP! in developing IT system and processes
organization. For example, following the NBP! experience, it
was considered appropriate to reorganize the IT Department
by creating a new direction related to project management and
organization of IT processes, which will allow supporting the
NBM’s strategic objectives.
In order to improve the human resources employment, to in-
crease the labor quality and productivity growth in the NBM,
the Netherlands Central Bank and the U.S. Treasury Department
provided technical assistance to optimize and improve human
resource management through analysis of the draft Regulation
on personnel assessment, description of the training planning
and long-term development stages.
2.11
National Bank of Moldova employees
and professional development
The human resources policy promoted by the NBM during 2011
sought to ensure the achievement of a climate that would allow
bank’s employees to work efﬁciently and to achieve optimum
results.
On December 31, 2011, the NBM payroll was completed at the
level of 98.9 percent (461 of 466 staff employees). There are 7
doctors of technical and economic sciences working within the
NBM.
In 2011, 51 employees of the bank were promoted. Persons
aged up to 40 years - 261 workers, or 56.6 percent of total are
the base of the Bank’s employees team.
During the reporting year, 125 employees of National Bank
of Moldova participated in foreign skills upgrade courses, and
211 employees participated at internal courses that contributed
to the improvement and modernization of the activity of the
National Bank of Moldova.
During 2011, the second edition of a survey of employees
has been organized, which helped at assessing the rate of
satisfaction of employees on different aspects of work, level
of involvement, etc.


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
93
2.12
Internal Audit
The internal audit is a separate and independent function, serv-
ing to evaluate the internal control system, to provide assurance
on the effective operation of risk management, and acting as a
catalyst for change.
In 2011, the IAD conducted 24 audits, of which 23 audits
have been planned and an additional mission was conducted
at the initiative of the Internal Audit Department (IAD). The
planned missions were insurance missions deﬁned by the statute
(regulations) of the internal audit, consisting of evaluating the
efﬁciency and effectiveness of internal control system and risk
management activity. The additional mission consisted in assess-
ing the integrity and efﬁciency of regulations portfolio of the
National Bank of Moldova. In addition to the aforementioned
audit missions, IAD staff participated as an observer in several
important processes of the bank, mainly: (i) annual inventory of
cash and other valuables in management, (ii) annual inventory
of assets, receivables and payables of the NBM, (iii) on-line
monitoring of high-risk operational processes – as a speciﬁc
procedure for preventing and combating fraud.
In 2011, there have been submitted 85 recommendations (in
2010 - 35) aimed at optimizing the procedural and operational
work, the controls, increasing operational efﬁciency of the NBM
resources. The increase in 2011 by 2.4 times of the number
of ﬁndings / recommendations was determined by the pre-
selection and evaluation of new audit areas.
Developing business consulting assistance, the IAD has devel-
oped in 2011 a program “Target” - training of IAD staff with the
participation of responsible employees from the NBM subdivi-
sions with Front, Middle and Back ofﬁce functions.
In this context, there has been organized and conducted a
seminar for the internal auditors of the licensed banks of the
Republic of Moldova on “Internal Audit. The objective of the
highest quality standards”.
The seminars were aimed at deepening the personnel’s aware-
ness of activity processes, associated risks, requirements of
applicable professional standards, internal auditor’s role.
At the same time, there have been continued the activities
speciﬁc for the development of processes, methodologies and
instruments of the internal audit, which mainly consisted of:


94
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
• development of fraud indicators (warning signs) for the
NBM areas that show signiﬁcant risks of fraud / corrup-
tion;
• setting the framework conditions for sound organization,
evidence and transparency of meetings, organized and
conducted within the National Bank, and ongoing moni-
toring of their conduct, settlement and reporting of actions
taken by the management;
• implementation of the electronic incidents evidence pro-
cess within the NBM;
• Data Matrix application – strengths and weaknesses of
internal control system;
• implementation of a new structure of the internal audit
report;
• conducting a feasibility study on implementing within
the NBM of the applicative software IDEA (Interactive
Data Extraction & Analysis), designed to increase the audit
quality / evidence.
2.13
Activity of the Council of Adminis-
tration of the NBM
During 2011, there were held 63 meeting of the Council of
Administration of the National Bank of Moldova, where 324
decisions have been examined and adopted with regard to the
activity of the ﬁnancial and banking sector, and acts have been
approved and modiﬁed on the following ﬁelds:
• Monetary and Foreign Exchange Policy of the NBM;
• Payments System Oversight Policy of the Republic of Moldova;
• Accounting Policy of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Banking Regulation and Supervision;
• Bookkeeping;
• Balance of Payments.
During 2011, 58 general decisions of the Council of Administra-
tion of the National Bank of Moldova have been submitted for
publication in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of Moldova.


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
95
2.14
Completion and amendment of nor-
mative acts in 2011
Foreign exchange regulation
In 2011, the National Bank of Moldova activities related to
foreign exchange regulation were focused on adopting and/or
amending its normative acts related to the activity of foreign
exchange entities and reporting to the NBM of banks foreign
exchange operations.
In 2011, there were approved amendments and additions to the
Regulation on foreign exchange entities. Such decision was
due mainly to the following:
• Ensuring optimization of reporting method to the NBM by
the foreign exchange entities
In this regard, there were provided forms for these reports,
the procedure for ﬁlling them out, and was established the
requirement for the foreign exchange entities to present this
report only in electronic form starting with 2012.
• The need to improve the method to inform the NBM about
the exchange ofﬁces of the licensed bank
In this respect, it was established that starting with 2012, the
banks shall present electronically the information related to
their exchange ofﬁces (e.g. notice about the opening of foreign
exchange ofﬁces of the licensed banks, changing working hours,
informative letters on suspension/resumption/termination of
the activity of the exchange ofﬁce).
• Changes in the structure of subdivisions of the licensed banks
by the Law no.241 on amending and supplementing certain
normative acts of 24.09.2010
The law in question has established the possibility of opening
within the banks branches of auxiliary ofﬁces, i.e. internal
structural subdivisions located outside the branch ofﬁce, without
a separate bookkeeping. Considering this fact, through the
amendments made to the regulation was clariﬁed the deﬁnition
of a licensed bank’s exchange ofﬁce under the national foreign
exchange laws, as well as a number of other relevant provisions
of the Regulation were adjusted.


96
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
• Improving the regulatory framework related to foreign ex-
change entities activity in preventing and combating money
laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing
The respective changes and additions were introduced, primar-
ily in the context of amendments made to the Law no.67 of
04.07.2011 to amend the Law no.190-XVI of 26.07.2007 on
prevention and combating money laundering and terrorism
ﬁnancing.
Among the amendments and additions can be highlighted the
following:
• there were set additional requirements to the document
issued by the licensed bank that conﬁrms the existence
in the account of the foreign exchange ofﬁce/branch of
money contributions to the equity capital, in particular
this document shall contain information about the source
of the funds;
• there were introduced provisions to help to ensure com-
pliance with the legislation in preventing and combating
money laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing related to the
measures of identiﬁcation of persons;
• there were embodied the provisions of the regulation re-
lated to the statement of refusal to perform the operation
by the foreign exchange entities;
• the term of keeping by the foreign exchange entities of
documents relating to transactions made and individuals
identiﬁed was reduced from 7 to 5 years, being stipulated
that upon the request of the Ofﬁce for prevention and
ﬁght against money laundering and the National Bank
of Moldova, the keeping term shall be extended to the
required period;
• there were introduced provisions related to the politically
exposed persons;
• there was speciﬁed the period within which the foreign
exchange ofﬁce and foreign exchange bureaus of hotels
will have its own program to prevent and combat money
laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing and the term within
which shall be designated the person responsible for en-
suring compliance with internal policies and procedures
of foreign exchange ofﬁce / foreign exchange bureaus of
hotels with the requirements of the relevant legislation;


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
97
• there were introduced amendments and additions to the
Recommendations on preparation by foreign exchange
ofﬁces and foreign exchange bureaus of hotels of programs
to prevent and combat money laundering and terrorism
ﬁnancing (e.g. there were introduced provisions for on-
going monitoring of transactions made by the clients in
order to identify the complex and unusual operations,
suspicious transactions, cashiers and other personnel ac-
cess to relevant customer identiﬁcation data, documents
related to the operations made and other relevant infor-
mation, establishment by the foreign exchange ofﬁces /
foreign exchange bureaus of hotels their own criteria for
determining the suspect nature of transactions, etc.).
The fulﬁllment of the provisions of Regulation on foreign ex-
change entities related to the submission of reports in electronic
form determined the need to regulate this process. In this
respect, was approved the Instruction on submission by the
foreign exchange ofﬁces / foreign exchange bureaus of ho-
tels of reports in electronic form to the National Bank of
Moldova. This Instruction establishes the procedure for submit-
ting to the NBM of reports in electronic form, the procedures
for recording the authorized persons to submit the reports in
electronic form, describes the procedure for transmitting the
reports in electronic form, establishes conditions for acceptance
by the NBM of these reports, contains provisions relating to
the correction of reports in electronic form, determines the
responsibilities of foreign exchange ofﬁces / foreign exchange
bureaus of hotels, etc.
In the context of transition to the IFRS along with the modiﬁ-
cations made to the Chart of Accounts of bookkeeping within
licensed banks of the Republic of Moldova, there were adjusted
the provisions of the Instruction on reporting foreign ex-
change transactions by the licensed banks and of the Instruc-
tion to the Report on correspondent accounts and other
placements of the licensed bank.
Also, in the context of optimization of reporting to the NBM
by the banks, due to the amendments to the Report on open
foreign exchange position of the bank, the Report on accounts in
foreign currency of residents and non-residents and the accounts
in national currency of non-residents was excluded.
Prudential regulation
In order to ensure a higher reliability in banks, during 2011
there have been undertaken a series of actions aimed at strength-
ening the domestic banking system. Thus, the improvement of
banking regulation and supervision has been further promoted.


98
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
To further strengthen the domestic banking system and to com-
ply with the new amendments to the Accounting Law, related
to the transition of banking system to the bookkeeping in accor-
dance with International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS),
there were made changes in prudential and ﬁnancial reporting
of banks to the NBM.
Thus, to provide a single reporting framework to apply IFRS by
banks, there were analyzed FINREP standard reports developed
by the European Committee for Banking Supervision. In this
context, given the speciﬁc reporting requirements established
by the National Bank of Moldova, there were determined FIN-
REP reports that licensed banks will be obliged to submit to
the National Bank of Moldova. Thus, there was developed
the Instruction on FINREP individual ﬁnancial statements
applicable to banks.
This Instruction came into force on January 1, 2012 and includes
some FINREP reports that will be presented in phases. Thus,
during the ﬁrst phase (starting with January 1, 2012) there
must be submitted reports containing basic information (FIN 1
and FIN 2) and some reports, other than the basic ones, that
provide additional information and details of the indicators of
basic information (FIN 3, FIN 5, FIN 7, FIN 9, FIN 10, FIN 13,
FIN 14, FIN 17, FIN 19, FIN 20, FIN 22, FIN 24, FIN 26, FIN
27).
Also, the Instruction on the elaboration and submission by
the banks procedure of the reports for prudential purposes
was developed, which modiﬁes the reports submitted until
December 31, 2011 by banks to the NBM, taking into account
the new requirements of the Chart of accounts of bookkeeping
within licensed banks of the Republic of Moldova, the coverage
of the required information by FINREP reports and the practice
in banks’ supervision activities.
It was also included a new report that reﬂects information about
the bank’s loan portfolio, while excluding other two reports for
loans, namely the report on loans and ﬁnancial leasing and the
report on expired and non-interest related assets.
At the same time, the Regulation on the open foreign ex-
change position of the bank in the context of transition to
the IFRS and avoidance of duplication of information in
different reports has been amended.
In order to eliminate some discrepancies between ﬁnancial and
prudential reporting in the banking system, the Regulation on
the classiﬁcation of conditional assets and liabilities was
expressed in a new wording. The provisions speciﬁed in this


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
99
Regulation will encourage further development of a strict regu-
latory approach to credit risk assessment.
In order to comply with the changes made to the Law on
Financial Institutions (i.e. Law no.241 on amending and sup-
plementing certain normative acts of 24.09.2010), there was
issued a new Regulation on outsourcing bank’s activities and
operations, which lays down the conditions for outsourcing
bank’s activities / operations, the requirements and procedure
for obtaining permission for outsourcing the activities of ma-
terial importance, the minimum content of the outsourcing
contract, the risk management requirements and the control of
outsourced activities / operations.
At the same time, there was developed the Regulation on
branches, representative ofﬁces and auxiliary ofﬁces that
establishes the conditions for opening of branches, represen-
tative ofﬁces and auxiliary ofﬁces of licensed banks from the
Republic of Moldova (hereinafter - banks), the procedure for
prior approval of opening of branches and representative ofﬁces,
for the notiﬁcation of opening of the auxiliary ofﬁces and the
procedure for closing the respective subdivisions.
As regarding the lending activity, the banks are subject to the
credit risk, i.e. the debtor may failure to reimburse the amount
due. In order to minimize this risk, the banks take certain
precautionary measures. The reduction of this risk may be due
to not only precautionary measures but also to the existence
of collateral serving for debt insurance and which may also be
long-term tangible assets. Thus, depending on the quality of
loans, the banks are entitled to take over the tangible assets
into possession or to purchase them in exchange for credit
reimbursement in accordance with the applicable law.
In this context, the Regulation on banks’ investment in long-
term tangible assets has been amended, being renamed as
Regulation on banks’ investments in tangible assets. There
were also established the requirements related to the possession
and reﬂection in the banks’ balance sheet of tangible assets
transmitted into possession or acquired in exchange for credits
reimbursement. These changes relate to the value at which
such assets are to be reﬂected on banks’ balance sheets, their
merchantability and other characteristics in accordance with
the International Financial Reporting Standard 5.
Within the process of granting foreign currency-linked loans, the
bank is exposed to a major credit risk, because the depreciation
of the national currency makes difﬁcult the repayment of such
a loan as the debtor will have to repay a larger amount in the
national currency. Thus, to avoid bank involvement in excessive


100
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
risks related to foreign currency-linked loans, the Regulation
no.3/09 on “large” exposures established the limits on the
size of exposures to individuals, including individuals perform-
ing an entrepreneurial activity or other type of activity, in the
amount of 30.0 percent of total regulatory capital, of which the
total net amount of exposures, other than mortgage, shall not
exceed 10.0 percent.
In order to minimize the risks related to the bank’s exposures
to its afﬁliated persons and given the opportunity of these per-
sons to inﬂuence the bank, there was amended the Regulation
no.1/09 on transactions with afﬁliated persons related to
the establishment of tighter limits on bank exposures to its
afﬁliated persons. Thus, the total amount of bank exposures
to its afﬁliated persons and/or groups of persons acting jointly
with afﬁliated persons shall not exceed 20 percent of the size of
the bank’s Tier I capital.
The bank’s capital is the main indicator of its ﬁnancial stability,
its sufﬁciency shall be kept at a level that would ensure the
ability to attenuate the negative inﬂuence of the risks to which
the bank is exposed in the conduct of its ﬁnancial activities.
In this context, given the international trends in this ﬁeld and
taking into account the speciﬁc of the local banking market,
it was decided to increase gradually the risk weighted capital
adequacy ratio, starting with June 30, 2012, the ratio shall be at
least 16.0 percent, starting with June 30, 2013, the ratio shall
be at least 18.0 percent and starting with June 30, 2014, the
ratio shall be of at least 20.0 percent.
At the same time, given that bank capital distribution, including
in the form of dividends, may lead to their de-capitalization and
that the bank capital represents the main indicator of ﬁnancial
stability, it was speciﬁed that the distribution of capital should
be made with regard to the income stated by a independent
auditing company. Thus, the Regulation on Risk Weighted
Capital Adequacy has been amended, aimed at strengthening
the domestic banking system capital.
A prerequisite for an effective functioning of the banking system
is the promotion to the position of administrator of those who
will contribute within their activity to an efﬁcient and prudent
management of banks, as well as to an adequate corporate
governance.
Thus, given the fact that bank administrators are vital for the
safe and stable functioning of the bank and can affect the risk
proﬁle of the bank, the Regulation on the requirements to
Bank’s administrators was amended. There were established
additional requirements to the administrators on their work


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
101
experience and reputation in business environment. Simulta-
neously, there were set new requirements related to the NBM
notiﬁcation in case of appointment to a position similar to the
function held by an administrator within the same bank and
in case of dismissal of an administrator or transfer to another
function, there were speciﬁed in detail the cases when the
conﬁrmation given to the administrator may lose its validity and
there were determined the corrective measures and penalties
that may be applied to an administrator in case of violating the
provisions of the Law on Financial Institutions.
In order to accumulate full information by the National Bank,
there was developed a questionnaire, which will be ﬁlled out by
the person proposed for the position of ofﬁce administrator. This
questionnaire includes a series of statements on the person’s
responsibility on the knowledge, experience and reputation in
this ﬁeld.
During 2011, the NBM has taken steps to improve the supervi-
sion and regulation of licensed banks in preventing and com-
bating money laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing, to minimize
the risks of illicit economic activities. Thus, the NBM approved
the Regulation on bank’s activity regarding prevention and
combat of money laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing and
the Recommendations on banks’ risk-based approach ac-
tions taking in relation to their customers in the context of
prevention and combating money laundering and terrorism
ﬁnancing.
These documents have the purpose of fulﬁlling the provisions
of Law on prevention and combating money laundering and ter-
rorism ﬁnancing and the international recommendations in the
ﬁled, in particular the documents of Basel Committee and the
International Financial Action Task Force (FATF) and establish
requirements for the development, organization, implementa-
tion and monitoring compliance by banks of their programs
on preventing and combating money laundering and terrorism
ﬁnancing, in order to minimize risks of money laundering and
terrorism, ﬁnancing. At the same time, these documents also
contain provisions relating to the structure of the program in
the ﬁeld, know-your-customer rules, including procedures for
customer acceptance, customer identiﬁcation measures, mon-
itoring of their activities and transactions, customer due dili-
gence, requirements for reporting the suspicious transactions to
the Ofﬁce for prevention and ﬁght against money laundering,
measures related to risk based approach to customers and their
transactions, etc.


102
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
2.15
Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation for
2011
General considerations
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova
no.548-XIII of 21.07.1995, the primary objective of the National
Bank of Moldova is to achieve and maintain price stability.
The basic functions and attributions of the NBM are stipulated
in the Law on the National Bank of Moldova. In accordance with
it, the NBM operations are performed in order to promote and
support the monetary and foreign exchange policy in the state,
to ensure the continuing stability of the banking system, national
currency issuance, foreign exchange reserves management, etc.
Accordingly, maximizing the proﬁt from its basic activity is not a
purpose itself for the NBM and cannot serve as an impediment
to achieve its primary objective and functions.
Proﬁt available for distribution
Considering the above, it should be mentioned that the ﬁnan-
cial results of the NBM are directly dependent on the need to
conduct monetary and foreign exchange operations in order to
achieve the given objective and fulﬁll the basic functions of the
NBM.
The evolution of the level of the proﬁt available for distribution
during 2007-2011 is shown in the Chart 2.59.
According to the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, 50.0
percent of the amount of proﬁt available for distribution is
allocated for the statutory capital increase and 50.0 percent is
transferred to the state budget.

According to the ﬁnancial situation, the NBM registered in 2011
a proﬁt available for distribution in the amount of 70.92 million
lei, which is characterized mainly by the following developments
as compared to the situation of 2010 (57.88 million lei):
The events that had a positive impact on the proﬁt available for
distribution:
• The increase of revenue from the amortization of the
discount on SS with about 83.0 million lei due to higher
weighted average interest rate (9.73 percent in 2011 as
compared to 6.18 percent in 2010).


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
103
• The increase of the revenue from the management of
foreign exchange reserves by about 51.0 million lei, due
to the increase of the average invested volume by about
USD 370.0 million, representing USD 1887.67 million in
2011 (USD 1518.71 million in 2010), and the increase of
the average proﬁtability rate of the investment portfolio
by about 0.173 percent (from 1.1157 percent in 2010 to
1.2887 percent in 2011).
The events that had a negative impact on the proﬁt available
for distribution:
• The increase of the expenses related to the sterilization
operations by about 58.0 million lei, mainly due to the
increase in the average weighted interest rate on issued
NBC by about 1.8 percent, reaching the level of 8.54
percent in 2011 (6.74 percent in 2010) .
• The increase of the expenses related to the required re-
serves by about 50.0 million lei, due to higher required
reserves ratios applied in 2011, which ranged from 8.0
percent to 14.0 percent (8.0 percent in 2010).
• The reduction of the interest-related income on medium-
term loans granted to banks (by about 31.0 million lei)
occurred as a result of loan repayment at maturity. Thus,
at the end of 2011, their balance was about 373.0 million
lei (on 31.12.2010 - 958.0 million lei).
The impact of the aforementioned events on the proﬁt available
for distribution, by areas of activity undertaken by the National
Bank during 2011, is presented in Table A.28.
The impact of the monetary policy on the ﬁnancial situation
of the NBM
The persistence of excess liquidity in the banking system prompt-
ed the National Bank to utilize the absorption of liquidity instru-
ments throughout the 2011 year.
During this time, the National Bank operated three upward
adjustments to the monetary policy rate, its level increasing
from 7.0 to 10.0 percent, and a reduction of 0.5 percentage
points in November 2011.
The expenses related to the sterilization operations by issuing
Certiﬁcates of the NBM increased signiﬁcantly in 2011, account-
ing for about 275.0 million lei compared to the level of about
217.0 million lei in 2010, due to higher interest rates on this


104
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
instrument under the inﬂuence of the increase of the NBM base
rate.
The average daily balance of sterilizations recorded the maxi-
mum level of 3934.3 million lei in February and the minimum
level of 2717.1 million lei in December 2011. Thus, the expenses
of the National Bank related to the monetary policy instruments
represent the cost to achieve the NBM objective of ensuring and
maintaining price stability.
All the instruments of monetary policy applied by the National
Bank during 2011 generated a net loss of 146.58 million lei,
representing an increase of losses with 51.11 percentage points
compared to 2010 (Table A.29).
The impact of state foreign exchange reserves management
operations on the ﬁnancial situation of the NBM
Foreign exchange reserves rose by 14.37 percent as of 31 De-
cember 2011 or by about USD 247.00 million compared to 31
December 2010 (from USD 1717.33 million to USD 1964.08
million), invested mainly in instruments in USD and EUR. The
increase of the share of foreign exchange reserves generated
an income growth related to the management of the foreign
exchange reserves and other foreign currencies operations by
52.21 million lei, reaching the level of 356.90 million lei (com-
pared to 304.69 million lei in 2010).
The impact of foreign exchange reserves management opera-
tions on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank is presented
in Table A.30.
A factor that inﬂuenced the income growth related to the man-
agement of the foreign exchange reserves was the increase of
proﬁtability of the investment portfolio. Thus, the weighted
average proﬁtability rate of term deposits increased from 0.3556
percent in 2010 to 0.5324 percent in 2011, and the proﬁtability
of securities issued by non-residents fell down from 1.6920
percent in 2010 to 1.6654 percent in 2011, as shown in the

Chart 2.60. The lower proﬁtability rates on securities during
2011 determined the decrease of the share of securities in the
investment portfolio (from 61.60 percent in January of 2011
to 54.53 percent in December of 2011) and the reinvestment
of due funds related to securities in other types of instruments:
term deposits, sight deposits, and current accounts with interest.
At the same time, by the Decision of the Council of Adminis-
tration of the NBM as of December 28, 2010, in force since
January 1, 2011, it was decided to increase the average term
of investment portfolio from 267 to 365 days, the maturity


Chapter 2. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
in 2011
105
diversiﬁcation contributed to the reduction of exposure to ﬁ-
nancial risks. Additionally, by the Decision of the Council of
Administration of the NBM as of December 1, 2011, was created
the portfolio of securities held to maturity that will not exceed
20.0 percent of the ofﬁcial reserve assets of the bank.
It should be mentioned that in accordance with best interna-
tional practices and the Law on the National Bank of Moldova,
the main criteria for selection of reserve assets are the safety of
basic amounts and liquidity. Thus, only after ensuring these two
criteria, the instruments proﬁtability in which the state foreign
exchange reserves are invested, shall be taken into account.
The development of the ofﬁcial foreign exchange rate of MDL
against the currencies in which the foreign exchange reserves
are held had a signiﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial situation of
the National Bank. Its dynamics and the average annual values
are shown in Table A.31.
Foreign exchange rate ﬂuctuations have led to the recording of
losses in 2011, the net unrealized losses from foreign exchange
rate differences amounting to 854.95 million lei, but these
losses did not affect the amount of unrealized proﬁt available
for distribution, whereas according to the Law on the National
Bank of Moldova, unrealized losses from foreign exchange rate
differences of 2011 were covered by the reserves created from
the unrealized income of previous years.
Capital and reserves
The activities conducted by the National Bank during 2011, in
order to fulﬁll its basic duties, had an impact on the bank’s
balance sheet structure (Table A.32).
The situation of capital and reserves is reﬂected in Table A.33.
As of December 31, 2011 the capital and reserves of the National
Bank recorded a negative level of 220.25 million lei, decreasing
by 742.14 million lei compared with the situation at the end of
2010. This decrease is due mainly to the record of unrealized
losses from foreign exchange rate differences from the revalua-
tion of foreign exchange stocks, amounting to 854.95 million
lei. These unrealized losses were covered by the reserves of
undistributed proﬁts made during the previous years, which
were not included in the proﬁt available for distribution.
The debit balance of the general reserve fund, which was
recorded at the end of 2008-2010, was not covered by the


106
Annual Report (NBM, 2011)
Ministry of Finance during 2009-2011 by issuing state securities
in accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova.
Given the primary objective and the basic tasks of the National
Bank, the negative capital and reserves recorded as of December
31, 2011, amounting to 220.25 million lei, cannot affect the
NBM’s principle of continuous activity and its operating capacity.
On December 31, 2011 the general reserve fund (GFR) was in-
creased by 35.46 million lei (or the debit balance of the GRF has
been reduced), representing 50.0 percent of the proﬁt available
for distribution as compared to 28.94 million lei recorded in
2010. The remaining balance of proﬁt available for distribution
was transferred to the state budget in April 2012.

Chapter A. Statistical tables
129
Table A.25: Investment limits and constraints imposed by the NBM in managing investment risk
Risks
Methods of reducing risks
Credit risk
*Investing in secure counterparties with high credit quality, set by international rating agencies: Standard&Poor’s, Moody’s and Fitch IBCA
(the minimum rating quoted by the three agencies shall be used).
*Establishing a minimum acceptable rating of counterparties authorized to conduct transactions.
*Establishing limits on countries, sectors, and investment instruments, counterparties/individual issuers, limits in terms of investment term.
*Daily monitoring of investment limits and compliance with credit limits.
*Credit risk quantiﬁcation, using default coefﬁcients determined by the Standard&Poor’s rating agency for each type of rating
and maturity.
Foreign exchange risk
*Establishing by the Council of Administration of the NBM a normative foreign exchange structure of the investment portfolio, covering
the currency risk through a proper correspondence of foreign exchange composition of assets and liabilities, and through an acceptable
investment horizon to service current external commitments and the implementation of the foreign exchange policy of the state.
*The normative foreign exchange structure is determined based on a comprehensive analysis of the structure of imports, foreign debt,
foreign exchange market turnover, structure of deposits in foreign currency, etc.
*Application of a policy to diversify the investment portfolio taking into account the SDR basket structure that is exposed to a lower
risk of exchange rate ﬂuctuations.
*Establishing by the NBM’s Investment Committee a foreign exchange strategic structure of the investment portfolio within the limits
not exceeding +/- 5% of the normative foreign exchange structure.
*Compliance with the normative foreign exchange structure and the foreign exchange strategic structure of the investment portfolio.
Liquidity risk
*Ensuring liquidity through on sight deposits and by investing in securities with high liquidity and low risk.
*Limiting the weighted average duration of the investment portfolio and monitoring its progress against benchmarks in dynamics
(deﬂection allowed shall not exceed +/- 20% of the normative average duration).
Source: NBM

Table A.36: Monetary policy decisons
Date of adoption by the Council
Decision
of Administration of the NBM
27 January 2011
- Required reserves ratio from funds attracted in MDL and foreign currency
increased from 8.0 to 11.0 percent of the base.
26 May 2011
- Required reserves ratio from funds attracted in MDL and foreign currency
increased from 11.0 to 14.0 percent of the base.
28 July 2011
- Base rate (on short term) increased from 8.0 to 9.0 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight loans increased from 11.0 to 12.0 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight deposits increased from 5.0 to 6.0 percent annually.
25 August 2011
- Base rate (on short term) increased from 9.0 to 10.0 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight loans increased from 12.0 to 13.0 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight deposits increased from 6.0 to 7.0 percent annually.
24 November 2011
- Base rate (on short term) decreased from 10.0 to 9.5 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight loans decreased from 13.0 to 12.5 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight deposits decreased from 7.0 to 6.5 percent annually.
29 December 2011
- Base rate (on short term) decreased from 9.5 to 8.5 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight loans decreased from 12.5 to 11.5 percent annually;
- Rate on overnight deposits decreased from 6.5 to 5.5 percent annually.
Source: NBM
